國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
美國經濟維持溫和擴張，勞動市場降溫與通膨黏著牽動聯準會政策 
經濟 
⚫ 2026 年第 2 季美國實質 GDP 季增年率終值上修至 2.2%， 惟低於第1 季
之 2.5%。成長主要由個人消費及固定投資帶動，其中消費貢獻 2.51 個
百分點；惟進口大增 12.6%，致淨出口拖累 1.10 個百分點。另實質私人
國內最終銷售成長 4.6%，顯示內需仍具韌性。 
⚫ 9 月美國非農就業僅增 2.9 萬人，遠低於前月及近一年平均，失業率升
至 4.2%， 薪資增幅續降 ， 顯示企業聘僱動能轉弱 。 惟勞動市場尚未明顯
惡化，後續仍須關注就業降溫對聯準會利率決策之影響。 
⚫ 8 月美國職位空缺數由 733.5 萬個降至 707.9 萬個，職缺率降至 4.3%，
顯示企業徵才需求略為降溫；惟聘僱人數增至 519.2 萬人。主動離職及
裁員規模同步下降，反映勞工轉職意願與企業人力調整趨於審慎，勞動
市場供需大致維持平衡。 
⚫ 9 月美國 ISM 製造業採購經理人指數為 54.5，連續第九個月維持擴張。
新訂單、就業及未完成訂單同步改善，顯示需求仍具韌性；惟生產、出
口及進口動能略為放緩，且原物料成本持續攀升，加上關稅政策、地緣
政治及供應鏈不確定性仍存，製造業仍面臨部分壓力。 
⚫ 8 月美國 PCE 物價指數月增 0.3%、年增 3.4%，核心 PCE 月增 0.2%、
年增 3.0%， 顯示通膨大致持穩但短期物價壓力略升 。 另一方面 ， 個人消
費支出月增 0.9%、實質消費成長 0.6%，帶動儲蓄率降至 4.1%，顯示家
庭需求仍具韌性；後續須關注消費熱度及通膨對聯準會政策之影響。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 近期聯準會官員立場趨於分歧，理事 Michael Barr 認為能源價格及 AI
投資熱潮可能延緩通膨降溫 ， 未來仍可能進一步升息 ； 惟John Williams、
Philip Jefferson 及 Michelle Bowman 等官員則主張暫無急於升息的必要 ，
傾向觀察後續經濟數據。加以 9 月非農就業明顯放緩，市場對 10 月升
息預期大幅降溫，焦點轉向 12 月會議。 
⚫ 台積電傳評估與馬斯克旗下 Terafab 合作赴德州設廠，雙方仍處討論階
段。合作可能由台積電主導營運，或由 Terafab 持多數股權、台積電提
供技術 。 若成真 ， 將擴大台積電美國布局 ， 並支援Tesla、SpaceX 及 xAI
晶片需求。

短評 
⚫ 美國經濟目前仍維持溫和擴張，內需與投資表現優於預期，短期衰退風
險有限 ； 惟近期新增就業明顯低於預期 ， 企業聘僱意願與職缺需求放緩 ，
勞動市場動能已有轉弱跡象，而通膨受能源價格、 AI 投資及關稅等因
素影響仍具黏著性。在經濟成長放緩與通膨尚未完全受控的環境下，聯
準會短期可能傾向維持觀望，惟若通膨再度升溫，不排除年底前仍有進
一步升息空間。

元 
區 
法國殖利率大幅上升，不利於投資及消費表現 
經濟 
⚫ 歐元區 9 月 HICP 通膨、核心通膨(不含食品及能源)分別上升 0.6、0.1
個百分點至 3.8%、2.5%。 
⚫ 歐元區 8 月失業率持平 6.4%。 
⚫ 歐元區第二季家庭儲蓄率下降 0.2 個百分點至 14.2%。 
⚫ 歐元區第二季房價年增率為 4%，較第一季降 0.6 個百分點。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 法國 10 年期公債殖利率升至 4.9%以上，與德國公債殖利率差距擴大至
125 個基點以上。 
短評 ⚫ 法國財政前景不明朗 ， 未來仍可能面臨殖利率進一步上升與財政壓力加劇的風險，暫時不利於消費及投資表現。 
日 
本 
景氣溫和復甦，但消費放緩與通膨升溫風險升高 
經濟 
⚫ 8 月工業生產月減 1.7%。 
⚫ 8 月零售月減 1.2%。 
⚫ 9 月東京核心通膨年增 2.7%。 
短評 
⚫ 儘管消費動能放緩與通膨壓力升溫 ， 但企業投資與生產回升趨勢仍具韌
性，經濟可望維持溫和復甦，惟日本央行進一步升息的可能性正逐步提
高。 
中 
國 
為刺激內需、引導產業走向，人行漸近式放寬政策工具 
經濟 ⚫ 9 月官方製造業 PMI、非製造業 PMI 分別上升為 50.1、50.2。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 人行於 9 月 29 日宣布下調抵押補充貸款(該貸款本質是將資金引導至政
府希望發展的領域)利率自 1.75%降至 1.50%，並擴大適用範圍。 
短評 
⚫ 未來若內需復甦力道不如預期 ， 不排除人行將進一步降準或擴大結構性
貨幣工具支持經濟 ； 至於全面降息則仍須兼顧人民幣匯率及中美利差等
因素，可能性相對較低。

國際經濟金融週報 
兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
115.10.5 
一、 美國—美國經濟維持溫和擴張，勞動市場降溫與通膨黏著牽動聯準會政策 
2026 年第二季美國實質 GDP 季增年率終值由前次估計之 1.5%上修至 2.2%，
惟仍低於第一季之 2.5%， 顯示經濟持續擴張但成長動能略有放緩 。 此次上修主要
反映投資、消費及政府支出優於原先估計。就成長結構觀察，個人消費支出仍為
主要動能，貢獻 2.51 個百分點，其中服務及商品消費分別貢獻 1.57 及 0.94 個百
分點；私人投資貢獻 0.82 個百分點，以設備投資及智慧財產權投資表現強勁，惟
庫存調整拖累 0.53 個百分點。對外貿易方面，出口成長 5.0%，但進口大幅增加
12.6%， 致淨出口拖累GDP 成長 1.10 個百分點 。 另反映內需基本面的實質私人國
內最終銷售成長 4.6%， 高於第一季之1.8%， 顯示民間需求明顯增強 。 物價方面 ，
第 2 季 PCE 及核心 PCE 物價指數分別上升 5.0%及 3.3%， 顯示通膨壓力仍偏高 。
整體而言，美國經濟仍由消費與固定投資支撐，惟進口快速增加及通膨黏著性，
仍為後續經濟前景的重要觀察重點。 
2026 年 9 月美國非農就業人數增加 2.9 萬人 ， 明顯低於上月修正後的13.3 萬
人，也低於過去 12 個月平均每月增加 4.5 萬人的水準；失業率小幅升至 4.2%，
整體仍維持在 3 月以來 4.1%至 4.3%的區間，反映勞動市場整體變化有限，惟新
增就業力道已轉趨疲弱。家庭調查方面，勞動參與率升至 61.8%，就業人口比為
59.2%，均較上月小幅上升；長期失業人數約 194.4 萬人，占總失業人口 27.1%，
大致維持穩定。產業別方面，主要產業就業變化有限，其中醫療保健增加 1.7 萬
人、營建業增加 1.1 萬人、製造業增加 0.9 萬人，金融活動則減少 0.7 萬人，政府
部門減少 1.7 萬人。薪資方面，私人部門平均時薪升至 37.81 美元，月增 0.1%、
年增 3.0%，平均每週工時維持 34.4 小時。另 7 月與 8 月新增就業合計下修 6 萬
人，反映前二月就業動能較初估疲弱。整體而言，9 月美國新增就業明顯放緩、
失業率小幅上升且薪資增速降溫，企業聘僱動能較先前轉弱；惟失業率仍維持於
近期區間，勞動市場尚未出現明顯惡化跡象，後續仍須關注企業聘僱需求是否持
續降溫，以及對聯準會利率決策的影響。 
2026 年 8 月美國職位空缺數(Job Openings) 由上月修正後之 733.5 萬個減少
25.6 萬個至 707.9 萬個 ， 職缺率由4.4%降至 4.3%， 顯示企業用人需求略有降溫 ；

惟聘僱人數由 514.6 萬人小幅增加至 519.2 萬人，聘僱率由 3.2%升至 3.3%。總離
職人數由 512.8 萬人減少至 507.0 萬人，離職率維持於 3.2%。就離職結構觀察，
主動離職(Quits)人數由 308.9 萬人降至 306.6 萬人，主動離職率維持 1.9%；裁員
與解僱(Layoffs and Discharges) 人數則由 170.2 萬人降至 164.1 萬人，裁員率由
1.1%降至 1.0%。產業別方面，職位空缺增加主要來自零售業、金融保險業、資訊
業及休閒餐飲業 ； 聘僱人數則以政府部門及製造業增幅較為明顯 。 整體而言 ，8 月
美國職缺數較前月下滑，反映企業徵才需求略有降溫，但聘僱活動仍維持穩定，
且主動離職與裁員規模同步下降，顯示勞工轉職意願及企業人力調整趨於審慎，
勞動市場供需仍大致維持平衡。 
2026 年 9 月美國 ISM 製造業採購經理人指數(Manufacturing PMI) 為 54.5，
較上月之 54.6 微降 0.1 點，惟已連續第九個月維持於 50 以上擴張區間，顯示製
造業景氣持續擴張。細項觀察，新訂單指數由53.7 升至 55.3，需求動能續增；生
產指數由 58.3 降至 56.7，雖仍維持擴張，但成長速度略有放緩；就業指數由51.2
升至 52.7，反映企業聘僱需求持續增加。供應商交貨指數由 59.3 微降至 59.0，交
貨延遲情況仍存；庫存指數則由 50.6 降至 48.6，企業備貨態度轉趨保守。此外，
價格指數由 71.1 升至 77.9，原物料及投入成本壓力升高；未完成訂單指數由51.8
升至 56.4，訂單去化變慢。另一方面，新出口訂單指數由53.2 降至 50.9，進口指
數亦由 52.5 降至 51.0，外需成長動能及企業備料需求略為放緩。整體而言，9 月
美國製造業活動持續穩健擴張，新訂單、就業及未完成訂單同步改善，顯示製造
業景氣仍具韌性；惟出口及進口增速放緩、投入成本壓力升高，加上關稅政策、
地緣政治風險及供應鏈不確定性仍存，後續發展仍值得持續關注。 
2026 年 8 月美國個人消費支出(PCE)物價指數月增率由上月之 0.1%升至 0.3%，
年增率維持於 3.4%； 扣除食品及能源後之核心PCE 月增率由 0.1%升至 0.2%，年
增率亦持平於 3.0%，顯示整體通膨大致持穩，惟短期物價漲勢較前月略有回升。
另一方面，個人所得月增 0.2%，可支配所得(DPI)月增 0.3%，實質可支配所得持
平；個人消費支出月增 0.9%，實質個人消費支出成長 0.6%，顯示民間消費表現
明顯回溫。由於消費增幅高於所得增幅，個人儲蓄率由前月之 4.6%降至 4.1%。
整體而言，8 月美國通膨表現大致持穩，但消費支出顯著回升，反映家庭需求仍
具韌性；後續仍須持續關注消費熱度、通膨走勢及其對聯準會貨幣政策路徑之影
響。

6

近期聯準會官員談話呈現鷹鴿分歧。聯準會理事 Michael Barr 認為，中東局
勢推升能源價格，以及 AI 相關投資熱潮帶動需求增長，均可能阻礙通膨回落至
2%目標 ， 未來仍不排除進一步升息的必要性 ； 惟 紐約聯準銀行總裁 John Williams、
聯準會副主席 Philip Jefferson 及金融監理副主席 Michelle Bowman 則強調，在 9
月升息後，當前並無急迫性立即進一步收緊政策，應先觀察後續通膨、就業及金
融市場數據變化，再評估是否有進一步調整利率的必要。受官員談話轉趨審慎，
以及 9 月非農就業表現疲弱影響，市場對 10 月 FOMC 會議再度升息的預期明顯
降溫，政策焦點逐漸轉向 12 月會議及年底前是否仍有一次升息空間。 
晶圓代工龍頭台積電傳出正評估與馬斯克旗下 Terafab 合作，在美國德州布
局晶圓廠，馬斯克也於社群平台 X 證實雙方正在討論，並表示 「目前僅在討論階
段 ， 但可能會有進展」 。 目前浮上檯面的合作模式包括由台積電持有並營運新廠 ，
SpaceX 或 Terafab 透過入股及承諾採購晶圓參與；另一方案則由 Terafab 掌握多
數股權 ， 台積電提供製程技術與晶圓廠營運經驗 。Terafab 旨在因應 Tesla、SpaceX
及 xAI 快速成長的 AI 晶片需求，首期投資規模達 168 億美元。若合作成真，不
僅可望成為台積電繼亞利桑那州後在美國的新布局，也凸顯先進製程與穩定晶片
供應的重要性，但目前具體合作方式、時程與投資內容仍未正式定案。 
二、歐元區—法國殖利率大幅上升，不利於投資及消費表現 
 歐元區 9 月 HICP 通膨 、 核心通膨(不含食品及能源)分別上升 0.6、0.1 個百分
點至 3.8%、2.5%， 主因國際原油價格上漲 ， 使能源通膨上升4.5 個百分點至 18.8%，
並透過肥料、物流成本上漲，間接推升食品通膨 0.3 個百分點至 1.4%。另外，服
務通膨上升 0.2 個百分點至 3.2%，近三個月服務通膨大致呈現橫向波動，部分反
映旅遊旺季因素影響。基於布蘭特原油持續位於每桶 90 美元以上，預期年底前
仍將對通膨帶來額外壓力，後續須關注能源漲價可能會透過運輸、物流等成本管
道傳導至核心商品及服務價格，進而提高核心通膨黏性。 
 其他經濟數據包含 ：(1)歐元區 8 月失業率持平 6.4%， 失業人數增加2.6 萬人 ，
主要是義大利勞動參與人數增加所致，不過 ，基於PMI 顯示近期德國勞動雇用需
求持續增溫，將有助於穩定勞動市場表現；(2)歐元區第二季家庭儲蓄率下降 0.2
個百分點至 14.2%，顯示通膨壓力削弱民眾購買力，致儲蓄暫時性減少；(3)歐元
區第二季房價年增率為 4%， 較第一季降0.6 個百分點 ， 主因法國中古屋面臨嚴格

### Page 8

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

7

環保法規，須負擔高昂翻修成本，使法國房價呈現連三季下滑所拖累。 
 近一週法國 10 年期公債殖利率升至 4.9%以上，與德國公債殖利率差距擴大
至 125 個基點以上，主要反映市場對法國主權公債可持續性疑慮升溫。儘管目前
政府計畫將 2027 年財政赤字縮限至 5%，惟政府在國會並無穩定多數席次，即使
能以行政命令強行通過預算 ， 也可能面臨倒閣風險 ；此外 ，市場對於2027 年 4 月
總統大選後，新總統能否取得國會足夠支持，推動削減赤字與控制債務的改革亦
缺乏信心。因此未來法國仍可能面臨殖利率進一步上升與財政壓力加劇的風險，
暫時不利於投資及消費信心。 
三、日本—景氣溫和復甦，但消費放緩與通膨升溫風險升高 
 8 月工業生產月減 1.7%，主要係受到該月地震及豪雨等天然災害影響，導致
部分工廠暫時停產。從細項來看，汽車業受停工影響最為明顯，產量下滑6.8%，
電子產品產量亦減少 1.9%。 惟生產機械產量連續三個月成長 ， 加上核心資本財出
貨持續增加，顯示企業投資需求仍相當強勁，有助於支撐後續工業生產回升。從
企業預期來看，製造商預期9 月產量將成長 3.2%，顯示生產活動可望迅速回溫，
在供應鏈逐步恢復、庫存回補需求持續，以及穩健的企業資本支出支撐下，預計
第三季工業生產仍可維持正成長，且成長動能可望延續至年底。 
 8 月零售月減 1.2%，雖然表現略優於市場預期，但仍顯示民間消費動能正在
放緩。此次疲弱主要原因來自耐久財銷售下滑，其中家電銷售因空調等產品在換
機需求告一段落後明顯回落，汽車銷售也隨政府刺激措施效應逐漸消退而連續第
四個月下降。相較之下，食品、服裝等非耐久財銷售雖同步走弱，但降幅相對有
限 ， 反映薪資成長仍對消費提供部分支撐 。惟不利天候與通膨壓力仍是主要風險 ，
8 月至 9 月持續豪雨已影響服裝等非必需消費，而除米價以外的食品及商品價格
持續上漲，也正侵蝕家庭實質購買力，恐限制消費者信心與消費復甦力道。 
 9 月東京核心通膨年增率升至 2.7%，雖然東京都免費托兒及免費水費政策造
成的基期效應貢獻約 0.5 個百分點，但通膨升溫主因仍是價格上漲已由能源與食
品擴散至整體經濟。受石油價格上揚帶動，企業開始加速將原物料及薪資成本轉
嫁給消費者，帶動商品與服務價格普遍走高；租金通膨更升至 2%，創 1994 年以
來新高，公共交通及醫療等項目也開始調漲。隨著東京的物價壓力逐步反映至全
國數據，日本央行進一步升息的可能性升高。

### Page 9

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

8

四、中國—為刺激內需、引導產業走向，人行漸近式放寬政策工具 
9 月官方製造業 PMI 上升 0.3 點至 50.1，為近三個月高點，新訂單、出口訂
單指數分別為 50.5、50，並帶動生產指數上升至 51.7，不過，庫存指數下降、就
業指數加速衰退、企業展望減弱，皆拖累 PMI 指數水準。此外，投入、產出物價
指數分別上升至 60.8、54，顯示成本壓力加劇，但售價調整速度依然較為遲緩，
不利於獲利表現。官方非製造業 PMI 上升 1.2 點至 50.2，其中建築業活動指數大
增 3.4 點至 50.3，脫離連續八個月的衰退區間，主要是政府加大財政刺激力度，
推動重大建設開工；非製造業的從業人員指數為 45.9，顯示就業情況仍顯嚴峻，
亦較不利於內需表現。 
鑒於內需動能疲弱，人行於 9 月 29 日宣布下調抵押補充貸款(該貸款本質是
將資金引導至政府希望發展的領域)利率自 1.75%降至 1.50%，並擴大適用範圍，
將水網、新型電網、算力網、新一代通信網、城市地下管網及物流網等建設納入
支持，有助於提振內需動能。未來若內需復甦力道不如預期，不排除人行將進一
步降準或擴大結構性貨幣工具支持經濟；至於全面降息則仍須兼顧人民幣匯率及
中美利差等因素，可能性相對較低。 
五、印度及東南亞—泰國、菲律賓出口均達 2 成以上的高速成長 
(1)新加坡：受服務業、零售暨其他商品業，以及食品價格漲幅擴大影響，8 月消
費者物價指數(CPI)年增率升至 2.3%、不包括住宿及個人交通費用之核心 CPI 年
增率升至 2.2%， 連續3 個月上升並創 2 年新高 。 受全球能源價格居高不下及氣候
條件不佳影響，預期新加坡進口食品、商品及服務成本將持續上升，惟國內生產
率成長及政府補貼政策將抑制部分服務業通膨，新加坡金管局(MAS)或在 10 月
「略微」緊縮貨幣政策。(2)泰國：8 月出口額年增 24.3%(346 億美元)，主要由工
業產品出口成長 27.1%所帶動，其中電腦及相關設備（年增 42.1%）、電話及零
件（151.2%） 及電路板 （19.2%） 增幅最為顯著，對美國出口年增48.7%、對中國
和日本分別成長 15.5%、19.6%。8 月進口額年增 25.1%(371 億美元)，以燃料 （年
增 42.8%） 、 資本貨物 （33.9%） 、 其他貨物 （27.4%） 、 原材料和中間產品 （21.3%）、
運輸設備（13.6%）為主。進出口相抵，8 月貿易逆差 24.8 億美元，較 7 月略收
窄，為連續第 11 個月出現逆差。(3)菲律賓：8 月出口額大增 27.8%(91 億美元)，
創兩年新高，主要得益於電子產品（年增 59.9%） ，尤其是半導體（73.5%）及黃

市場焦點
美國
公債回購
• 美國財政部9月9日宣布，將於翌日回購最高60億美元的10至20年期美債，低於部分市場期待
的80億至100億美元規模 。民間持有美債規模達 31.8兆美元，相較之下 ，本次60億美元回購
僅約占民間持債規模 0.02%，對整體影響有限 。此外，約6成的美債期限不足 5年，意味未來
再融資需求仍高，若財政赤字未明顯收斂，長端殖利率上行壓力仍存。
日本央行
• 日本第二季GDP年化季增率上修至 1.4%(優於初值1.1%)，儘管伊朗戰爭引發原油供應緊張給
經濟帶來壓力，但GDP增速依舊高於0.7%潛在經濟成長率，雖然經濟表現支持日本央行(BOJ)
逐步實施貨幣政策正常化 ，但更關鍵的催化劑來自日美兩國協調干預匯市 ，遏制日圓極端貶
值的政治壓力，彭博經濟研究預期日本央行將在9月會議上加息25個基點。

美國
通膨
• 美國8月生產者物價指數 （PPI）年增5.4%，略高市場預期的 5.3%，核心PPI年增4.6%，符合
預期。8月消費者物價指數（CPI）年增3.35%、核心CPI年增2.4%，均符合市場預期，但核心
CPI月升幅0.3%，高於市場預期的 0.2%。數據公佈後，FedWatch顯示市場預期9月升息機率，
從前一周的50%攀升至86%。

美國8月通膨數據(CPI)
• 美國8月通膨數據：8月美國CPI月增0.4%、年增3.4%，核心CPI月增0.3%，核心服務通膨升溫至0.5%，顯示通膨降溫仍不
均衡，能源價格上漲為主要推手，市場預估8月核心PCE月增約0.3%、年增約3.8%，仍高於Fed 2%目標，搭配強勁就業數
據，9月升息1碼的壓力升高。不過，本次通膨主要來自能源、需求及AI投資帶動，與2021～2022年供給型通膨有所不同。
• 市場反應與展望：CPI公布後，2年期美債殖利率一度上升，惟長天期殖利率反而走低，顯示市場關注焦點並非單純升息，而
是Fed能否維持抗通膨政策可信度。整體而言，升息未必等同Risk-off，若經濟與企業獲利維持韌性，股市仍具支撐。
資料來源： investing.com，2026.9.14

燃料成本料推動8月運輸成本上升

能源成本回升推升PPI，通膨壓力牽動Fed政策空間
• 能源成本回升，PPI重新升溫：美國8月PPI月增0.4%、年增5.4%，增幅均明顯高於7月，主要受到商品價格上漲帶動；其
中最終需求商品價格月增1.1%，能源價格上升4.2%，柴油更是大漲逾2成，成為商品價格上升的重要來源，惟最終需求服
務價格僅略增0.1%，顯示本次PPI升溫仍以能源及商品價格為主。
• 成本壓力仍在，利率政策不確定性升高： 扣除食品、能源及貿易服務後的核心 PPI月增0.3%，微低於7月的0.4%，但年增
4.7%，顯示價格壓力仍未完全消退；其中運輸與倉儲服務價格上升2.3%，反映部分成本仍具黏著性，致使市場對Fed利率
政策的重新定價明顯升溫，進而推動股、債市短線波動擴大。
• 市場觀察：由於本次PPI升溫主要來自能源成本 ，但服務價格壓力整體仍相對溫和 ，因此尚不足以視為通膨全面升溫 ，然

能源價格若持續走高 ，將提高Fed維持較高利率的壓力 ，建議投資人短線宜降低長天期債券與高估值成長股的追價 ，債券
可優先配置短中天期、高品質債券，股票則可關注能源、金融及具定價能力企業，提高投組對利率與成本波動的承受度。
資料來源：U.S. Bureau of Labor Statistics，Producer Price Indexes – Aug,  2026，2026.9.11

• 財政部對美債的回購規模不如預期：美國財政部9月9日宣布，將於翌日回購最高60億美元的10至20年期美債，主要鎖定流

動性較低的舊券，以改善長天期債券市場流動性。雖然本次回購高於先前設定的每次至少40億美元，但低於部分市場期待的
80億至100億美元規模。消息公布後10年期公債殖利率逼近5%，30年期殖利率亦突破5.3%。
• 回購規模在高額赤字背景下，難以有效拉低長天期公債殖利率：美國今年公債發行規模較2025年增加11.8%，民間持有美債
規模達31.8兆美元、年增8.2%，相較之下，本次60億美元回購僅約占民間持債規模0.02%，對整體影響有限。此外，約6成
的美債期限不足5年，意味未來再融資需求仍高，若財政赤字未明顯收斂，長端殖利率上行壓力仍存。反觀新興國家基本面
持續改善，且當地債市有貨幣潛在升值空間，可作為全球債券配置的分散來源。

• 地緣政治動盪推升油價，但全球經濟依然展現韌性：美國和伊朗的衝突已經進入第7個月，能源價格上升及地緣政治風險仍
對全球經濟與市場信心形成壓力，但AI投資熱潮持續推升科技需求，使全球經濟展現優於預期的韌性。其中，亞洲仍是全球
成長動能最強的區域之一，2026年亞洲（不含日本）經濟成長率預估達4.7%，明顯高於全球平均2.9%及成熟市場1.6%。在
內需與科技出口支撐下，新興亞洲基本面相對穩健，具備較佳的中長期配置條件。

• AI資本支出延續，台韓成為核心受惠市場：AI資本支出持續擴張，2026年全球雲端AI資本支出預估達7,960億美元，並帶動
AI半導體市場至2030年突破7,500億美元。亞洲掌握全球半導體供應鏈核心，台灣受惠於先進製程與先進封裝需求，韓國則
受惠於HBM記憶體需求，台韓可望成為AI基礎建設擴張的主要受惠市場；同時，中國與印度則提供較穩定的內需及經濟成
長支撐。2026年亞洲企業盈餘成長率預估達72.13%，顯著高於全球平均，建議新興亞洲以台韓科技股為主要成長配置，並
搭配中國、印度市場分散區域風險。

短債供給增加未引發融資緊張，短天期債券防禦價值提升
• 回購市場維持穩定，短債供給暫未造成壓力：美國回購市場今年以來維持平穩，儘管9月短債償付與企業繳稅可能帶來短
暫資金波動，但SOFR與IORB利差仍處相對正常區間 ，顯示目前準備金與短期流動性尚稱充裕 。此外SOFR/IORB差值對
短期美債淨發行增加的敏感度明顯低於2025年底，反映流動性環境偏寬鬆。
• 貨幣基金成長放緩，供給吸收能力受到限制：零售資金流入偏弱，使貨幣市場基金資產增速落後 ，2026年迄今僅增加約
325億美元，遠低於2025年同期的2,050億美元；即便Q4季節性回升，新增資金對短債供給的承接能力仍可能有限。
• 後市觀察：年底前短債淨發行規模預估約3,250億美元，後續需關注短債供給、企業繳稅及財政部長端公債回購對資金面

的影響。建議投資人配置上可提高 1～3年期美國國庫券或短天期美債 ETF比重，以取得票息收益並降低長端殖利率波動
對債券價格的影響。
資料來源：US Treasury，FRBNY ，Federal Reserve ，2026.9.9

風險偏好升溫與債券供給增加，推升期限溢價與長債殖利率
• 全球期限溢酬上升，長債面臨重新定價：全球債券殖利率同步走高，不僅反映各國政策利率預期，也受到全球期限溢酬上升
推動；投資人對持有長天期政府債券要求更高補償 ，顯示長債殖利率上行已具有跨市場的結構性因素 。Bloomberg
Economics的模型亦顯示，全球期限溢酬是近年G7長債殖利率走高的重要共同因素。
• 風險資產吸引資金，放大長債供需壓力：AI投資題材推升股票市場、信用利差仍相對偏窄，加上大宗商品走強，使股票、公
司債及商品與政府債券形成資金競爭；在政府融資需求持續增加的情況下 ，若債券需求未同步擴張，政府公債需提供更高殖
利率才能吸引資金。
• 後市觀察：長端利率已成為股債配置的重要變數 ，若殖利率因財政供給與風險偏好維持高檔 ，長天期債券及高估值股票均可

能承壓；建議以短中天期美債及高品質投資級公司債為核心 ，待長端殖利率充分反映期限溢酬後，再分批拉長債券存續期間。

日本央行升息箭在弦上，但缺乏連續升息條件
• GDP修正支持升息 ，但關鍵點在配合日本和美國政府遏制日圓疲軟： 日本第二季 GDP年化季增率上修至 1.4%(優於初值
1.1%)，儘管伊朗戰爭引發原油供應緊張給經濟帶來壓力，但GDP增速依舊高於0.7%潛在經濟成長率，雖然經濟表現支持日
本央行(BOJ)逐步實施貨幣政策正常化 ，但更關鍵的催化劑來自日美兩國協調干預匯市 ，遏制日圓極端貶值的政治壓力 ，彭
博經濟研究預期日本央行將在9月會議上加息25個基點。
• 缺乏連續大幅加息的支撐力道： (1)經濟衝擊風險：日圓升值效應已對經濟產生緊縮效果 ，彭博預估可能拖累實際 GDP成長
達0.3%。(2)財政負擔過重：若政策利率升至 1.75%以上，將大幅增加政府利息支出 ，擠壓高市內閣的財政刺激空間 。(3)企

業投資放緩：私人資本支出仍顯疲軟(季比下降0.9%)，顯示經濟復甦基礎尚未完全穩固。
• 小結：日本央行在政治、匯率與經濟基本面交集下 ，9月升息已成定局，但考量財政負擔與全球金融穩定後 ，其後續加息步
伐應偏向謹慎與循序漸進 ，密切關注9月日本央行會議後聲明 ，若央行強調循序漸進而非連續緊縮 ，市場波動相對可控；反
之，若釋放過度鷹派訊號，則需防範市場拋售風險。由於市場資金對日圓套利交易依賴度仍高，日圓任何短期內的急速升值，
都極易觸發全球風險資產的連鎖去槓桿效應。

中國8月通膨回升，惟需求端疲弱
• 8月份 CPI及PPI：消費者物 價指 數 (CPI)月增 0.4%(前月月減 0.1%)、年增 0.8%(前月 0.5%)，生 產者物價指數 (PPI) 月增
0.4%(前月月減0.7%)、年增3.8%(前月3.5%)。通膨走揚主要受到能源及通訊設備成本上升影響。
• 觀點：中國自2023年以來，內需市場面臨房地產崩盤 、青年失業率飆升以及公共債務攀升等困境 。今年年初以來，通膨雖
然由負轉正，不過從細項得知，因國際油價飆升、AI硬體設備面臨漲價壓力，本波通膨回升主要是受到供給端影響。外部成
本壓力恐進一步壓抑中國內需消費，中國要在真正意義上走出通縮環境可能還需要更多官方政策發力。彭博預估，在需求未
出現明顯改善的情境下，中國央行需要近一步放寬貨幣政策，預測年底前可能降息10個基點。

資料來源：Bloomberg，截至2026.9.9。

AI產業獲利亮眼，但需留意變現能力和資本效率
• AI獲利增速領先大盤，銷售成長成為獲利關鍵：AI板塊在2026年第一與第二季分別實現52.8%與59.7%的高獲利成長，受高
基期影響，未來四季度平均獲利成長預計放緩至 34.4%，但仍大幅優於非 AI板塊12.3%。預計2026年第三季至2027年第二
季，AI平均銷售成長率將達 27.2%，為非AI成分股(5%)5倍以上 。隨著未來利潤率擴張放緩 (預計未來 6個季度平均僅提升
1.66%，低於過去12個季度的3.52%)，持續的銷售成長是維持獲利的關鍵。
• AI供應鏈內部發展並不均衡： 半導體最為強勁，2026Q3至2027Q2預期銷售成長65.8%，獲利大增112.1%且淨利潤率大幅
擴張8.86%，為產業鏈核心引擎。雲端巨頭表現穩健，營收與獲利增速維持約26%，基礎設施軟體與硬體也有15%~20%營

收與獲利成長 。但應用端軟體成長相對乏力 ，未來四季度獲利預期增速僅 4.3%與6.5%，表現低於標普500非AI股平均水準。
• 資本強度創新高 ，但自由現金流承壓： AI資本支出佔營收比重自 2024年Q1的8.9%一路攀升至 2026年Q2的20.1%，預計
2026年下半年將達到23%的高點。資本支出激增導致自由現金流與資本支出之比(FCF/Capex)從2.48倍滑落至0.69倍，自由
現金流利潤率亦從21%降至13.9%。隨著現金逐漸消耗，企業可能需透過債券市場進行發債融資 ，以維持龐大基礎設施投資。
• 小結與投資建議：AI板塊依然是美股獲利驅動力，但隨利潤率提升空間受限、自由現金流受資本支出壓制，投資邏輯已從全
面暴漲轉向注重變現能力與資本效率。投資建議聚焦上游硬體與半導體，建議持續優選具備高定價能力與實質訂單支撐的半
導體與雲端基礎設施龍頭，留意資本支出佔比過高且自由現金流急劇惡化公司，優先選擇現金流充沛、融資成本低企業。
標普500指數盈利成長：AI vs. 非AI
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.7

標普500指數剔除AI後利潤率或下降
AI股票：自由現金流 vs. 資本支出

• 歐洲央行9月升息一碼：歐央將三大政策利率各上調25個基點，存款利率升至

銀行業擴大收益來源， 建議投資人可以適度配置，增加投組多元性。

近五年美國8月CPI年增率
燃料成本料推動8月運輸成本上升
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.14
• 美國8月通膨數據：8月美國CPI月增0.4%、年增3.4%，核心CPI月增0.3%，核心服務通膨升溫至0.5%，顯示通膨降溫仍不均衡，
能源價格上漲為主要推手，市場預估8月核心PCE月增約0.3%、年增約3.8%，仍高於Fed 2%目標，搭配強勁就業數據，9月升息
1碼的壓力升高。不過，本次通膨主要來自能源、需求及AI投資帶動，與2021～2022年供給型通膨有所不同。
• 市場反應與展望：CPI公布後，2年期美債殖利率一度上升，惟長天期殖利率反而走低，顯示市場關注焦點並非單純升息，而是

Fed能否維持抗通膨政策可信度。整體而言，升息未必等同Risk-off，若經濟與企業獲利維持韌性，股市仍具支撐。

前應詳閱基金公開說明書。

資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。
5. 本資料所載內容取自本公司相信可靠之資料來源，且為特定日期之判斷，本公司不保證其即時性、精確性及完整性，亦不提供或嘗試遊說客戶做為投資之判斷依據。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정삼성전자(005930) 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

삼성전자 (005930)

Company Brief 
반도체/디스플레이 
Analyst 김선우 
sunwoo.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 우서현 
seohyun.woo@meritz.co.kr 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정  
3Q26E 영업이익 106.1조원, 원화강세 불구 견조한 수준 기록 전망 
삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익은 106.1조원으로 낮아진 시장 기대치 (104조원)를 소폭 
능가할 전망이다. 해당 분기동안 급격한 원화강세로 인해 실적 측면의 비우호적 환경
이 조성되었지만, 동사는 강건한 판매 정책에 기반해 메모리 판가가 산업 평균을 재
차 능가하며 견조한 실적 개선이 가능했다. 
동사 메모리 사업부의 3Q26 영업이익은 109조원으로 예상되며, 이중 DRAM과 
NAND는 각각 80조원 (OPM 78.5%) 과 29조원 (OPM 68.9%) 을 차지할 전망이다. 
DRAM과 NAND 출하량은 기존 예상치를 소폭 하회하는 +3.4%/+4.2% QoQ을 기
록하겠지만, 판가가 무려 +20.1%/19.0% QoQ 상승하며 실적 개선을 이끌겠다. 전
분기에 이어 ‘DS 특별경영성과급’ 충당금이 반영되며 손익에 일부 영향을 미치겠지
만, 동사 특유의 ‘수익성 극대화’ 판가 정책이 산업 우위 구도를 조성한다. 한편 LSI/
파운드리는 영업손실이 1.5조원을 기록하며 적자가 지속될 전망이다. 가동률 개선에 
기반한 사업 회복이 진행중이지만, 테일러팹 가동과 함께 비용 요인이 지속 출회할 
전망이다. 한편 반도체 외 기타 사업부의 험난한 실적 추이는 지속될 전망이다. SDC 
1.1조원 영업이익을 제외하고, DX (세트) 부문의 경우 MX -1.9조원, DA/VD -7,400
억원으로 영업적자 확대되리라 예상된다. 당사가 지난해 말부터 주장했듯, 세트 사업
은 메모리 등 부품원가 비중이 급격히 상승하는 가운데 향후 ‘판가 인상 → 판매량 
축소’의 ‘내구재화’ 변모 속 적자 최소화 노력만이 불가피할 전망이다.

1Q27 일부 모바일 DRAM 판가 +50% 폭등 예상. 적극적 매수 권고 
AGI를 향한 경쟁적이며 구조적 서버 수요 증가 불구, 스마트폰 중심의 B2C 응용처
는 유일한 판가저항처로 남아있다. 하지만 당사의 채널체크에 따르면, 메모리 업체들
의 지속적인 생산 전환 (LPDDR → 서버 DRAM 및 HBM)에 기반해 주요 고객 향 
모바일 메모리 판가는 무려 1Q27부터 50% 이상 QoQ 상향될 전망이다. 주요 고객
은 내년 20주년 플래그십 스마트폰을 제작하는 상황에서 판가 폭등을 감내해야하는 
상황이다. 최근 메모리 사이클 개선 속도에 대한 막연하며 진부한 우려가 존재하나, 
향후 1~2개월간 메모리 업체들에 대한 2027년 실적 추정치 상향과 함께 ‘성장·저평
가·주주환원 매력’이 동시에 발생할 전망이다. 동사의 2027년 영업이익 추정치를 기
존의 562조원에서 632조원으로 13% 상향조정하며, 시장 눈높이 역시 지속 상향되
리라 예상한다. 한편 배당 중심으로 발표됐던 동사의 주주환원의 경우, 모든 주주에게 
더 실효적인 ‘자사주 매입·소각’ 방안이 병행되는 방향으로 변모하리라 예상된다. 투
자의견 Buy와 적정주가 50만원을 유지한다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 7

## Document 145: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 기대삼성바이오로직스(207940) 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 기대단기 부담_2a8cdc97

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 기대삼성바이오로직스(207940) 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 기대단기 부담_2a8cdc97.pdf

목표주가 2,000,000

(26.10.02) 
1,354,000 
제약업종

◼ 3Q26 컨센서스 부합, 4Q26 성장폭 확대 전망 
◼ 5공장 Ramp-up 지속, 신규 수주 회복 여부에 주목 
◼ 투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지 
목표주가는 DCF 방식으로 산출(WACC 8.1%, 영구성장률 3.5% 적용)

3Q26 Preview: 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 전망 
3Q26 매출액 1조 3,380억원(+6.4% yoy, +1.3% qoq), 영업이익 5,890억원(-
7.0% yoy, +0.4% qoq, OPM 44.0%)으로 컨센서스에 부합하는 실적을 전
망함. 3분기 평균 원/달러 환율이 전분기 대비 약 5% 하락하고, 지난 5월 
파업에 따른 생산 차질 영향이 반영되나, 5공장 PPQ 물량의 매출 인식이 
시작되며 yoy, qoq 외형 성장은 이어질 것으로 예상함.  
파업 당시 생산이 지연된 물량은 3Q 중 추가 생산되어 4Q26부터 매출에 반
영될 전망임. 5공장은 다수 프로젝트의 PPQ가 진행 중이며, Ramp-up에 
따라 매출 기여도 점진적으로 확대될 것으로 예상함. 이에 4Q26에는 파
업 이연 물량 반영과 5공장 매출 확대에 따라 실적 성장폭이 확대될 전
망임. 2026년 연간 매출액과 영업이익은 각각 5조 5,310억원(+21.4% 
yoy), 2조 4,780억원(+19.8% yoy, OPM 44.8%)으로 전망함.

신규 수주 회복 기대, 중장기 성장 기반은 확대 중 
관세 관련 불확실성으로 둔화됐던 고객사와의 수주 논의가 재개되고 있음. 
10월 2일 유럽 소재 제약사와 935억원 규모의 CMO 계약을 추가 체결
했으며, 4Q26 신규 수주 회복 여부에 주목함.  
PolyPeptide 인수는 연내 거래 종결을 목표로 진행 중이며, 인수 완료 
시 펩타이드 분야로 CDMO 포트폴리오가 확대될 전망임. 6 공장도 연내 
착공을 목표로 준비 중임. 
한편, 노사 리스크는 단기 불확실성 요인임. 노조는 10/26부터 3주간 2
차 총파업을 예고함. 1차 대비 예정된 파업 기간이 길어진 만큼 실제 파
업 진행 시 생산 차질 규모가 주요 변수임. 다만 파업 개시 전까지 협상 
가능성이 열려 있어, 향후 노사 협상 진행 경과를 확인할 필요가 있음.

## Document 162: AI灾难将在一年内发生？Anthropic、OpenAI私下推演最坏情景

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI灾难将在一年内发生？Anthropic、OpenAI私下推演最坏情景.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-09
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Anthropic 、 OpenAI 等 AI 公司正私下推演重⼤事故发⽣后的应对⽅案。业内⼈⼠担忧，未
来 6 ⾄ 12 个⽉可能发⽣重⼤事件，引发公众反弹与监管收紧。企业正提前向美国国会议员普
及 AI 知识，希望影响危机发⽣后的政策制定。
摘要：
⼈⼯智能⾏业正在为⼀场可能改变监管格局的重⼤事故做准备。
据 Axios 最新报道，Anthropic 、 OpenAI 等公司的⾼管正私下推演，如果 AI 引发严重现实伤害，
美国社会和政界将如何反应。
这些推演重点关注⼤规模⽹络攻击等极端情景，包括⾦融服务、互联⽹连接乃⾄电⼒和供⽔系
统中断。业内⼈⼠担忧，⼀旦发⽣由不安全 AI 导致的重⼤事故，原本就对这项技术持谨慎态度的
公众可能进⼀步转向反对， AI 企业及其⾼管也将⾯临更⼤的舆论压⼒。
报道称，多名 AI ⾏业⼈⼠认为，未来 6 ⾄ 12 个⽉内可能发⽣重⼤事件。企业⾼管因此开始提前与
美国国会议员沟通，希望在危机真正发⽣后，影响美国政府制定的相关法律和政策。
AI 公司担忧重⼤事故引发公众反弹

据 Axios 报道， AI 企业正在评估重⼤事故发⽣后可能出现的政治和社会反应。相关情景包括失控
的 AI 智能体集群突破内部测试环境的限制，或恶意⾏为者利⽤现有模型实施攻击。
企业担忧，如果 AI ⾸次造成重⼤现实伤害，原本对这项技术持谨慎态度的公众可能进⼀步转向反
对。
Anthropic CEO Dario Amodei 、 OpenAI CEO Sam Altman 等⾏业领导者，以及被批评不愿加强
AI 监管的美国总统特朗普，都可能承受更⼤的舆论压⼒。
企业提前沟通，为事故后的监管做准备
据报道， AI 企业正在通过红队测试推演最坏情景，同时加快向美国国会议员普及 AI 相关知识。
企业⾼管认为，⽬前推动监管⽴法的条件有限，但重⼤事故可能改变政策讨论的⽅向。因此，
他们希望提前参与相关讨论，影响美国政府届时可能制定的法律和政策。
部分⺠主党⼈⼠提出禁⽌超级智能，或暂停先进 AI 开发等主张。另⼀些提案获得了两党⽀持，甚
⾄得到部分⾏业⼈⼠认同，例如要求先进 AI 系统设置紧急关闭开关。不过，专家也质疑，全⾯关

闭所有 AI 系统在实际操作中是否可⾏。
与此同时，⼤量开放权重模型已经可以免费下载和使⽤，增加了⻛险治理的复杂性。许多⽹络
安全专业⼈⼠认为，利⽤ AI 对抗恶意 AI 是应对这⼀问题的途径之⼀。
监管收紧⾯临现实约束
即使重⼤ AI 事故引发强烈反弹，美国政府在制定应对政策时也可能⾯临多重制约。

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

写评论
图⽚
AI 基础设施建设已与全球经济深度交织。如果放缓相关建设，可能对本已脆弱的经济造成冲击。
这意味着，政府在考虑加强 AI 监管时，还需要⾯对相关经济影响。
政治分歧同样是企业关注的问题。部分 AI ⾏业⼈⼠预计，⺠主党可能在中期选举后推动更严格的
AI 限制措施，但即使⺠主党同时控制众议院和参议院，党内分歧仍可能影响政策推进。
Axios 援引⼀名⺠主党助⼿的观点称，真正发⽣危机时，华盛顿两党可能暂时搁置分歧。新冠疫
情和 2008 年⾦融危机期间的合作经历，被视为可能的参考。
⽬前， AI 重⼤事故是否会在未来 6 ⾄ 12 个⽉内发⽣仍不确定。可以确定的是， Anthropic 、
OpenAI 等企业已开始推演事故发⽣后的政策情景，并试图提前参与相关监管讨论。

## Document 164: AI芯片越造越多，数据中心电却不够用：半导体出货预期面临考验

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI芯片越造越多，数据中心电却不够用：半导体出货预期面临考验.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

AI 基础设施投资狂潮正遭遇严重供需错配：芯⽚产能扩张快于数据中⼼建设，电⼒短缺、
供应链瓶颈和审批延误导致⼤量项⽬延期。摩根⼠丹利预计，到 2028 年美国数据中⼼电⼒
缺⼝将达芯⽚需求所需电⼒的约三分之⼀， AI 资本开⽀回报与芯⽚销售前景⾯临考验。
摘要：
AI 基础设施投资热潮正⾯临⼀场严峻的供需错配危机。
据彭博专栏作家 Chris Bryant 的分析，尽管科技⾏业正以前所未有的速度向 AI 计算基础设施砸
钱，数据中⼼建设却因电⼒短缺、供应链瓶颈、监管审批延误等多重障碍严重滞后。摩根⼠丹
利分析师在近期报告中警告，" 没有⾜够的电⼒来⽀撑所有⼈的计划，⽽正在⽣产的半导体数量
已超过可供安置的场所。 "这⼀判断与⻢斯克此前的类似表态相互印证。
这场错配的直接市场影响不容忽视：美国数据中⼼开发商⾯临的电⼒缺⼝，预计到 2028 年将达
到摩根⼠丹利所预测同期芯⽚销售所需电⼒的约三分之⼀。这意味着，股市对半导体出货量的
乐观预期存在被证伪的⻛险，并可能对芯⽚制造商、数据中⼼开发商及其租⼾的财务表现形成

连锁冲击。
半数⼤型项⽬尚未破⼟动⼯
数据中⼼建设的实际进展远落后于市场预期。据市场情报机构 Currence 的数据，今年宣布的⼤
型数据中⼼项⽬中，有⼀半尚未开⼯。
建设障碍涵盖多个层⾯：设备与劳动⼒短缺、社区反对、施⼯禁令、许可证延误，以及电⽹接
⼊周期漫⻓、燃⽓轮机产能不⾜等能源供给问题。 BloombergNEF 的数据进⼀步显⽰，按容量计
算，美国数据中⼼在建管道中，近半数由⾸次涉⾜该领域的开发商主导，⾏业经验的匮乏令外
界对问题能否快速解决缺乏信⼼。
与此同时，借贷成本上升、贷款机构趋于审慎，也令项⽬融资的不确定性加剧。当前各机构对
未来数年内最终可建成计算能⼒的预测分歧显著，折射出市场对供应链与监管瓶颈何时疏通的
普遍疑虑。
合同保护存在漏洞，开发商与租⼾⻛险各异
对于数据中⼼的房东和贷款⽅⽽⾔，合同条款在⼀定程度上可提供成本超⽀保护，并约束租⼾
在延误情况下的退出权利。然⽽，据彭博同事 Paul Davies 指出，这类保护机制因合同⽽异，租
⼾资质与担保⽅质量同样⾄关重要。

伦敦初创公司 Nscale Ltd. 的 IPO ⽂件揭⽰了潜在⻛险：其与 Anthropic 签订的价值 446 亿美元的
计算合同，在供应链延误情形下 " 仅提供有限救济 " 。若 Nscale 未能按时交付， Dario Amodei 旗
下的 Anthropic 有权 " ⽆需承担责任 " 地终⽌合同。
Oracle Corp. 同样⾯临类似压⼒。该公司不得不向投资者保证，其在新墨西哥州 Jupiter 项⽬数据
中⼼的租约进展正常，尽管该项⽬因天然⽓管道许可问题受阻， Oracle 随后发出的不可抗⼒通
知并未提振市场信⼼。据悉，即便可以递延租⾦， Oracle 仍须向项⽬融资⽅承担部分付款义
务。
软银系 SB Energy ： 500 亿美元估值遭遇质疑
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
83 收藏 分享到 :
软银集团旗下 SB Energy 的案例，集中体现了当前 AI 基础设施投资泡沫化的隐忧。该公司在 IPO
招股书中披露，其拥有 8.8 吉⽡的签约计算项⽬管道，其中⼤部分⾯向 OpenAI ，但⽬前仅有极⼩
部分处于在建状态，且尚⽆任何数据中⼼投⼊运营。这⼀现状令其 500 亿美元的估值遭到投资者
质疑。
值得注意的是，英伟达正以⼀笔 1050 亿美元的担保介⼊其中 —— 为租⼾ OpenAI 的租赁义务提供
背书，以⽀持 SB Energy 的建设推进。这⼀举措本⾝即表明，英伟达对数据中⼼建设滞后可能影
响其处理器销售前景保持⾼度警惕。英伟达在最新财报中亦明确提⽰，⼟地、电⼒、建筑或资
本的短缺均可能制约其未来财务表现。
超⼤规模云⼚商⾯临 " 囤货 " 困境
对于亚⻢逊、微软等 " 超⼤规模 " 云计算巨头⽽⾔，芯⽚与数据中⼼之间的供需错配正演变为⼀项

切实的财务⻛险。这些公司通常在数据中⼼投⼊使⽤前 " 数⽉ " 才采购服务器和⽹络设备，因此不
愿提前囤积⽆法通电、且⾯临技术过时⻛险的芯⽚。
⽬前， AI 企业持有的尚未投⼊使⽤的资产规模已接近 3500 亿美元。为弥合新站点上线前的产能
缺⼝， BloombergNEF 建议，可将现有数据中⼼中的旧款芯⽚替换为英伟达最新、计算效率更⾼
的型号。然⽽，这将进⼀步推⾼超⼤规模云⼚商本已庞⼤的资本⽀出，并增加折旧压⼒。
另⼀条潜在出路是将数据中⼼容量转移⾄可再⽣能源更为充裕的国家，这或将为欧洲提供更多
参与 AI 基础设施建设的机会。
英伟达市值逼近 6 万亿美元，但⻛险已在财报中现⾝
尽管英伟达市值已接近 6 万亿美元，投资者⽬前似乎并不过度担忧 Jensen Huang 旗下公司为其
⾼利润处理器寻找买家的能⼒。然⽽，英伟达在最新财报中已明确将数据中⼼建设瓶颈列为潜
在⻛险因素，这⼀表述本⾝即值得市场关注。
当前，供应链瓶颈在短期内确实为部分参与者带来了丰厚回报：⻢斯克旗下 SpaceX 正以⾼昂价

格出租计算能⼒，卡特彼勒和 Bloom Energy Corp. 的现场发电设备订单堆积，顶级电⽓⼯程师
的薪酬甚⾄⾼达 75 万美元。但这种局部繁荣能否持续，取决于整体基础设施建设能否跟上芯⽚
出货的节奏。
正如 Chris Bryant 所⾔，在经历了数年的狂欢之后， AI 盛宴或许正在演变为⼀场⾼⻛险的 " 抢椅

## Document 174: Allianz_2026-10-09_Toasted, not burnt France s bond sell-off and the road to the 2027 election_99a3202d

Source PDF: all report pdf/Allianz_2026-10-09_Toasted, not burnt France s bond sell-off and the road to the 2027 election_99a3202d.pdf
Report source: Allianz Trade
Date: 2026-10-09
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

In Summary 
• France is the hardest hit in the global bond sell-off, mostly due to its own fiscal and political 
weakness. Since the Hormuz crisis began, 10-year OAT yields have risen 150bps (US +115bps, 
Italy +132bps, Germany +83bps) as the prolonged energy crisis exacerbates fiscal concerns in 
a politically fragile environment. Franc e’ fiscal deficits have remained above 5% of GDP for 
four consecutive years. Debt has risen from 98% of GDP in 2019 to over 119% in Q2 2026, and 
the 2026 deficit is now expected at -5.4% against a -5% initial target. 
• A "short Europe" trade amplifies the move and spreads it to the rest of the Eurozone while 
the ECB looks the other way. Foreign banks and non-banks hold 36% of OATs and Japanese 
investors about 5% (EUR137bn), exposing France to the repatriation trade and price-sensitive 
investors. Speculative shorts, hedge -fund asset -swap unwinds and thin liquidity feed a 
negative loop, w ith market depth more than 50% below normal and the OAT spread over 
Bunds peaking at 150bp s. The speed reflects a positioning shock, not a pure repricing of 
fundamentals. Long -term investors are sitting out ahead of the elections, given budget 
uncertainty and the sensitivity of OATs to political fragility. Spanish and Italian spreads are 
about 20bps wider, bank CDS are widening and French and Italian three -month bills are 
converging toward the ECB's marginal lending rate. Fragmentation is far from 2012 or 2020 
levels, but the ingredients are in place. The ECB has not yet intervened and may limit  itself to 
verbal intervention, while continuing QT and pushing through two more hikes (+50bps).  
• Baseline: A watered -down budget, but the fiscal arithmetic stays heavy for France .  
We expect GDP growth of +0.5% in 2026 and +0.9% in 2027. The Assembly votes on the budget 
on 20 and 27 October, and we expect a budget by year -end through ordinances or Article 
49.3, keeping the 2027 deficit at -5.3% of GDP. This should contain fiscal slippage and stabilize 
financial conditions, with OAT spreads in the 120 -150bps range. Higher rates  (+100bps) will 
feed through over the next three to six months, adding EUR25bn (0.8% of GDP) in gross 
interest expenses for households, non -financial firms and the government combined. The 
debt-stabilizing deficit is -2.7% of GDP, which requires savings of more than  EUR80bn, plus 
EUR15-20bn a year to stop social and interest spending from widening the deficit. This takes 
credible fiscal measures (better tax mix, higher retirement age) and growth -enhancing 
reforms. 
• Adverse scenarios: France-centered crisis or Eurozone crisis? The Middle East situation is far 
from contained; oil prices could stay above USD100/bbl for another six months , leading to a 
further global bond sell -off hitting France the hardest, tightening financial conditions and 
jeopardizing deficit reduction. Domestically, opposition parties could take down the 
government or slippage surprises resurface despite a passed budget, tightening conditions in 
the same way. In this context, GDP would stall at +0.0% in 2027 and spread s reach 150 -
175bps. The ECB would talk up and potentially launch repo intervention. In our view, it would 
take a substantial Eurozone -wide contraction (France -0.5% in 2027 ; Eurozone -0.2%; OAT 
spreads at 175 -200bps) for the ECB to tweak or stop QT, launch open market and repo 
intervention and stabilize funding markets (as OATs and BTPs make up 50% of euro repo 
collateral). 
• Looking ahead, the 2027 elections are the next test, and TPI is a late and uncertain 
backstop. With the presidential election on 18 A pril and 2 May 2027 (legislative elections 
likely to follow), we see four scenarios: i) a President with an absolute majority and allies, who 
delivers fiscal consolidation; ii) a President short of a majority who seeks alliances  (our 
baseline, with the 2028 deficit at -4.9% and spreads above 100bps) ; iii) a President short of a 
majority without alliances  and iv) a political and fiscal crisis, for instance from unfunded tax 
cuts or spending hikes, with spreads above 200bps and widespread contagion. Only in t his 
last scenario would the ECB activate the Transmission Protection Instrument (TPI). The risk is 
a lack of political will rather than of instruments, and redenomination risk is the tail case.

Allianz Research | 9 October 2026 
Toasted, not burnt: France’s bond sell-off  
and the road to the 2027 election

### Page 2

France is the country most affected in the global bond sell-off

Since the start of the Hormuz crisis,  yields on 10 -year French government bonds (OAT) have risen by 150bps  
(vs 115bps for the US, 132bps for Italy and 83bps for Germany), the highest jump among developed economies. 
The feedback loop between rising oil prices and interest rate expectations  has met structurally vulnerable public 
finances whose credibility has been increasingly undermined by growing political fragility since the 2024 dissolution 
of the National Assembly. France’s debt-to-GDP ratio has surged from 98% in 2019 to over 119% in Q2 2026, with 
over 121% of GDP expected in 2027 (vs 83% to 87% in the Eurozone). Meanwhile, the  weakening economy (+0.5% 
GDP growth expected this year)  is leading to a fourth consecutive  year of fiscal slippage : The government now 
expects a deficit of -5.4% of GDP against the -5.1% targeted in the 2026 Budget. With Moody's upcoming review on 
23 October, France is now at risk of losing its AA rating (currently Aa3 by Moody’s vs A+ by S&P and Fitch Ratings, 
i.e. already one notch below); current pricing, if sustained, would even imply a BBB rating.

Table 1: Macroeconomic and fiscal forecasts for France in 2027

Government Allianz Research 
Real GDP growth (%) 1.0 0.9 
Inflation (CPI. %) 1.8 2.2 
Public balance (% GDP) -5.0 -5.3 
Primary balance (% GDP) -2.1 -2.5 
Debt service costs (% GDP) 2.9 2.8 
Public debt (% GDP) 121.7 121.8

Sources: French government, Allianz Research

France is the first victim of the Japanese repatriation trade.  More than other European sovereigns, France's 
investor base is exposed to price -sensitive players. The most volatile group, foreign banks and non -banks, holds 
36% of French government bonds. Domestic banks and non-banks, whose holdings fluctuate less, account for 32%, 
while non -price sensitive investors (domestic and foreign central banks) make up only 32%. Other European 
countries have either a stronger anchor in the domestic market (Italy  with 66% vs 47% for France ) or with foreign 
central banks ( Germany with 26% vs 16 % for France as Bunds as the primary FX reserve vehicle). Among foreign 
holders, Japanese investors play a significant role , holding around 5% of outstanding French government bonds 
(~EUR137bn). As interest rates rise in Japan, these positions become less attractive relative to  domestic holdings. 
At best they are no longer rolled over, at worst they are actively liquidated. In the recent bond sell -off France 
became the first victim of the J apanese repatriation trade, even though it plays only a minor role compared with 
the US as an overweight in Japanese investors' benchmark allocations .

Figure 1: France’s risk premium is very sensitive to changes in political fragility

### Page 3

Fiscal concerns, political fragility and investor base have created the vulnerability ; sentiment and positioning 
determine the market reaction. A "short Europe" trade has built up for some time in markets and France is identified 
as the most vulnerable link (Figure 2). A negative feedback loop is now at work. A bearish narrative around political 
fragility and fiscal risk is amplified by very large speculative short positions, sales from Japanese institutional 
investors, hedge fund unwinding asset swap positions and a l imited presence of buyers, with market depth more 
than 50% below normal. In such conditions , even small trades can move prices sharply. OAT yields have spiked 
rapidly, with the 10-year spread vs the Bund widening to a peak 150bps, rising twice as fast as during the Eurozone 
crisis. Realized OAT spread volatility has jumped to the 90th percentile. These market dynamics point to massive 
repositioning rather than a repricing of fundamentals . But as long as a stabilizing force of buyers is missing, this 
volatility can persist.

Figure 2: A Short Europe trade has built up with pressure on EGB spreads and EUR -USD

Sources: LSEG Datastream, Allianz Research

As c ontagion has switched side s, the European Government Bond (EGB) market could move from country -
specific fragility to general fragmentation. The OAT sell-off has already spilled over to other EGBs. Unlike 15 years 
ago, when France fell victim to contagion from the periphery, it is now the other way round. Spanish and Italian 
bond yields rose significantly, while German yields fell. Their 10-year spreads versus the Bund widened by around 
20bps. This decoupling within the EGB space is accompanied by widening bank CDS spreads, especially in France. 
Meanwhile, Euro funding market is under elevated stress. French and Italian three-month bills are converging 
toward the ECB's Marginal Lending Facility rate, the effective ceiling for Eurozone money-market rates. A break 
above the MLF would signal a disruption in funding  markets. The ingredients for a broader fragmentation of 
monetary conditions are thus in place, even if the current magnitude  remains far from those of  2012 or 2020. 
However, monetary transmission is also a function  of time: If these stretched conditions persist for an extended 
period, an intervention of the ECB will be more likely , e specially if we see a persistent decoupling of yield 
movements between core and periphery that can feed fears of redenomination risk.

What’s next?

The 2027 budget bill is facing stiff opposition at the National Assembly, putting the planned deficit reduction in 
jeopardy. Presented to the cabinet on 1 October, the draft budget (PLF) and the social security finance bill (PLFSS) 
for 2027 target a public deficit of -5.0% of GDP, down from -5.4% in 2026. The budget is essentially about preventing 
the deficit from getting worse rather than a fiscal adjustment. The government has announced  EUR43bn of new 
fiscal-measures (1.4% of GDP) , split between EUR25bn (58%) in spending restraint (including slowing spending 
compared to expected trends such as healthcare spending, central government cuts, social spending restraint, local 
authorities cuts) and EUR18bn (42%) in new revenues.

First, opposition parties are already pushing back against most of the big revenue raisers measures, including the

### Page 4

cut to the tax allowance on pensioners’ income (EUR1.4bn), the taxation of sick leave (0.5bn) and cutbacks on 
funding to local governments (EUR2.5bn). Second, the persistence of elevated energy prices and tighter financial 
conditions could pressure GDP growth next year, denting expected government receipts. The government expects 
GDP growth at +1% in 2027, while we e xpect +0.9% but with increasing downside risks. The next key milestones to 
watch will be 20th and 27 October when the National Assembly votes on the Draft budget part I (revenues) and 
the Social Security finance bill (Figure 3). The debt stabilizing fiscal deficit stands at -2.7% of GDP, half of today’s 
level, which entails an improvement of the public deficit of EUR83bn from 2027 on top of around EUR15-20bn of 
additional savings to find each year  just to prevent the deficit from rising  (to contain rising social security and 
interest spending).

Figure 3: France’s key political & budget milestones

Note: legislative elections are not yet scheduled – the National Assembly’s term runs to 2029 unless the next president dissolves 
it. Sources: LOLF art. 45, Constitution art. 47, Assemblée nationale budget calendar of 15 Jul 2026, rating agencies, Allianz  
Research

We lay out different scenarios of what could happen between now and the elections (Figure 4). Starting in the 
short-term, we envisage three broad possibilities: i) a watered-down budget passed before year-end which plans a 
stabilization or a slight improvement of the deficit, ii) a political crisis  with opposition parties taking down the 
government and iii) a re-intensification of the energy crisis.

Figure 4: Economic, political and market scenarios October 2026-April 2027

Source: Allianz Research 
In our baseline scenario, we  expect the French government will manage to pass a watered-down budget while 
the energy crisis eases,  allowing the deficit to remain in check in 2027 (-5.3% of GDP) Opposition parties do not

### Page 5

have an incentive  to soften their stance with the Presidential elections approaching on 18 April and 2 May 2027. 
But they also do not have an incentive to create a political crisis by taking down the government. Notably, the 
National Rally (Rassemblement National, RN) has expressed a preference for implementing a budget rather than 
relying on the ‘’Special Law’’ mechanism that was used in early 2025 and early 2026 In this environment, we expect 
the government to manage to pass a mini budget, by year -end, of limited fiscal effort just to keep the deficit in 
check (at -5.3% of GDP), either through executive ordinances – a budgetary mechanism never previously utilized 
under the Fifth Republic – or by triggering Article 49.3.  
In this baseline scenario, OAT spreads should enter a phase of stabilization but contagion risk remains. After the 
clean-up of derivative positions, we see 10 -year OAT spreads trading in a pre -election range of 120 -150bps. But 
the evolution of the French spread will not be isolated , with the potential to trigger a renewed contagion move.  
The French budget and election situation feeds the “short Europe trade”, and this negative sentiment dominates 
country-specific fundamentals. We also do not believe that stabilizing the French situation alone will be enough to 
bring the EGB market back into a convergence trade. Now that dislocations have emerged, EGB spreads are likely 
to become the new playground for speculative (leveraged) players looking to exploit them. With this comes 
elevated rates and FX  volatility and the risk of money market stress. We expect the ECB in this case to limit itself 
first to verbal intervention like a change in wording from "monitoring" to "ready to act" or "disorderly market 
dynamics". We see the tipping point at a 10-year OAT spread above 150bps. 
In a more adverse scenario where the energy crisis does not ease and/or France slips into another political crisis, 
a crisis emerges, either France-centered, or with spillovers and contagion to other Eurozone countries.  Under a 
scenario of renewed tensions in energy markets, the global markets selloff would intensify. Financial conditions 
would tighten more in France, weakening the economy and putting in jeopardy the deficit reduction. Alternatively, 
the government could be taken down in Parliament, triggering a grave political crisis and leading to tighter 
financial conditions. Even under our baseline scenario of a watered-down budget, fiscal slippages could re-emerge 
– for example if the economy is weaker than expected, or if the fiscal adjustment fall s short of what is planned  
(e.g. if revenue -raising measures disappoint). Additionally, under a Special Law, the risk of wider deficit could 
increase if continued through the presidential elections  in 2027, which could lead to France -centered crisis or a 
Eurozone crisis if tightening financial conditions in France spill over to other Eurozone countries . In a France -
centered crisis we would expect the French economy t o enter a mid-sized recession as tighter financial conditions 
would weigh on domestic demand (see next section) , with GDP at +0.0% in 2027. In a Eurozone crisis , we would 
expect French GDP to contract -0.5% and the whole Eurozone economy -0.2%.  
In this adverse scenario, we could see the 10-year OAT spread rising to 1 75-200bps. This would create 
widespread contagion in the EGB market and in the banking sector. In this case, it will clearly be up to the ECB not 
to let the situation escalate into a euro crisi s 2.0. Unlike in the euro crisis, when the escalation was fueled by a lack 
of instruments while the political will was there, this time the deployment of the instruments (TPI, OMT) could fail 
due to a lack of political will and institutional inertia. It wo uld be particularly precarious if markets began to price 
redenomination risk, i.e. the possibility of a country leaving the Eurozone. But we see this scenario as a tail risk. If 
the EGB and money markets move towards general fragmentation, we expect the ECB to set operational steps (QT 
tweak, temporary operations) at OAT spreads around 175bps, but only alongside broader contagion with BTP 
spreads around 150bp s and Spain and Portugal widening together. For France, a QT tweak would represent a 
relief of EUR4.8bn on average per month, equivalent to around 20% of monthly issuances since June. But the 
problem is that “Europe short traders” can also do this math and adjust accordingly. At this point the ECB would 
not only operate in the secondary bond market but a lso need to stabilize funding markets as in  French OAT and 
Italian BTP represent 50% of the EUR repo market collateral. The TPI activation would be late in the process and 
require OAT spread around 200bps with widespread and persistent contagion.

In 2027, the presidential (and likely legislative) elections  could re -escalate economic and market pressures.  
We envisage four scenarios, which we detail more in Figure 5. In a nutshell, under our baseline assumptions, the

### Page 6

newly elected President will not secure a n absolute majority when she or he provokes legislative elections.  
Nevertheless, the President will have the strongest group at the National Assembly and will benefit from strong 
popular support. In this environment, we would expect the President to negotiate with some opposition parties to 
pass a budget putting the deficit on a downward trajectory. Nevertheless, deficit reduction will be limited ( -4.9% 
GDP deficit expected in 2028) amid strong aversion for rapid fiscal tightening in Parliament and public opinion. The 
10-year spread will likely remain above 100bps. In an adverse scenario of political and fiscal crisis, for instance 
triggered by the enactment of tax cuts and/or spending hikes  not credibly funded, a France-centered or Eurozone 
crisis scenario would re-emerge. In the latter case, the ECB would likely intervene with the TPI with French spreads 
breaking 200bps and Italian spreads above 175bps.

Figure 5:  Economic, political and market scenarios after April 2027

Source: Allianz Research

In the short term, a significant easing of global yields and elevated French spreads hinges on three factors:   
1) a substantial easing of the energy crisis, which would reduce inflationary pressures, limiting or even ending 
further central bank hikes. 2) Strong and credible fiscal -consolidation measures passed by decree, though this 
remains unlikely before the presidential election. One option used previously is  reducing healthcare spending, but 
the amount of savings is limited to EUR5-10bn (Table 2). 3) A cooling US economy could moderate the global bond 
sell off, though this too seems less likely before 2027.

Table 2: Measures the French government can implement by decrees to bypass parliamentary vote

Measure Can government act without new parliamentary vote? 
Cancel already authorized State spending Yes 
Freeze/cancel some budgetary appropriations Yes 
Adjust medicine reimbursement within regulatory powers Yes 
Adjust healthcare tariffs/payment parameters Yes, within statutory framework 
Tighten implementation of existing social schemes Often yes 
Change parameters of social contribution rebates  Sometimes yes 
Abolish/reduce a statutory benefit Generally, no 
Permanently change tax rates No 
Fundamentally redesign social-contribution exemptions Generally, requires legislation 
Change the ONDAM target itself Generally, Parliament/LFSS

Sources: French government, various, Allianz Research 
In the longer-term, only a sizeable fiscal -consolidation program can ease French spreads sustainably.  
Ideally, a combination of the following would have the most benefits:

### Page 7

Credible fiscal adjustment (the least growth -hurting). On the revenue side, fiscal consolidation should rely on a 
more efficient tax mix to limit negative growth effects. An increase of  VAT rates would bring large receipts without 
hurting potential growth. Freez ing income-tax thresholds should be preferred to increase social contributions to 
limit the negative impact on labor demand and labor supply amid already high labor taxation in France. Some 
inefficient and costly tax rebates and loopholes could also be cut down. But most of the fiscal adjustment should 
be operated through expenditure cuts. An increase of the effective age of retirement – for instance through a rise 
in the number of years of contribution – would bring substantial savings and increase tax receipts. Substantial 
savings could also be enacted on public agencies and local governments’ administrative costs. In parallel, the 
government could launch a comprehensive spending review to improve efficiency  of public expenditure, 
benchmarking what has been done in the past in countries such as Canada and Sweden.  
Growth-enhancing reforms. The government could implement structural reforms which will likely support growth 
in the medium -term, reducing the need for further painful fiscal adjustment later on. A pension reform would not 
only bring higher receipts and increase savings; it would also stimulate labor supply and potential growth 1. Other 
growth-enhancing measures include a revamp of school education met hods, scaling back excessive regulation, 
incentivizing higher-skilled education and hiring through a reform of the taxation system.

The trickle-down effect of tighter financial conditions

The ongoing bond market problem could increasingly become an economic problem through tighter financial 
and credit conditions, but the economy has buffers to sustain a short -term financial shock. Higher sovereign 
interest rates have started to filter through on to consumer, mortgage and corporate funding costs, and the pa ss-
through will continue through 2027. This will tighten the credit and financial cycle, through higher borrowing costs 
on new lending, higher refinancing bills and tighter access to credit, and ultimately weaken consumer and 
investment spending. Our BIS-built Financial condition index (FCI) for France shows that overall financial conditions 
have tightened sharply recently, but from a relatively loose level (Figure 6, left). They are now close to their long -
term historical averages. Besides, the credit impu lse for households and corporations remain s supportive to date 
(Figure 6, right). During the Eurozone crisis of 2011 -13, the credit impulse turned sharply negative but GDP growth 
held up. While the latest ECB Bank Lending Survey (BLS) points to the credit impulses weakening in the next couple 
of quarters, this does not necessarily mean that GDP growth will weaken sharply. The French economy has buffers 
to withstand a negative finance shock, provided it does not last too long and does not get worse. In parti cular, 
households have high savings and corporates’ cash reserves have been rebuilt recently, though many firms struggle 
with elevated bankruptcies.

Figure 6: France’s financial conditions index (left); credit impulses & GDP growth (right)

rates would almost double households’ net interest income in 2026 to -EUR16.3bn from -EUR8.5bn in 2025, or the 
equivalent of 0.8% of disposable income (see Figure 5). Contrary to US or UK households, French households tend 
to lose when interest rates increase. We calculate that the interest earned on assets would reach EUR24.5bn while 
the interest paid would rise to EUR40.7bn.

Figure 7: Impact of a +100bps shock on interest rates for French households

Sources: LSEG Datastream, Allianz Research

For French non-financial companies, around 50.6% of debt is due to reset or mature within the next 12 months, 
well below the level in Italy, Spain, the UK and the US, where 78 to 90% is due for refinancing. However, France 
starts from the largest corporate debt stock in Europe in this sample, at around EUR2.27trn. This means that even 
with a lower refinancing share, around EUR1.15trn of debt still needs to be repriced within a year, more than in 
Germany, It aly o r Spain. In addition, the pressure is amplified by the starting level of borrowing costs. French 
corporates already face an effective interest rate of 6.1%, the highest among the European peers shown here. Under 
a +100bp s interest-rate shock, the effective pass -through would amount to roughly +89bp s, adding around 
EUR10bn to annual interest expenses. Relative to gross operating surplus, this represents an additional burden of 
about 2.1% of GOS, the highest impact across similar peers, and could shave roughl y -0.7pp off gross margins (see 
Figure 6). For the French government, a +100bps increase in the weighted average interest rates would mean +0.3% 
of GDP in 2027 (EUR 11.7bn) and +0.7% of GDP by 2035. Indeed, France has a rather long average maturity of its 
debt (>8.5 years) which protects it from very high volatility on its interest expenses.

强，银⾏、农业股表现活跃。

影视传媒 /AI 语料： AI 应⽤密集落地，短剧爆发式增⻓点燃涨停潮
这是当⽇午后资⾦最集中的⽅向。影视传媒板块收涨 5.09% ，短剧 / 互动游戏板块涨 5.38% ， AI 语
料板块涨 4.83% ，主⼒资⾦净流⼊居市场前列。
个股端批量涨停：中⽂在线 20.01% 涨停、芒果超媒 19.98% 涨停，上海电影、⻰版传媒、吉视传

现 9 连板。
催化逻辑有三条清晰线索。
中邮证券认为，微短剧《办法》进⼀步强化备案审核、内容质量、版权保护及平台责任要求，
本质上是对供给侧进⾏规范与筛选，有望优先惠及头部制作⽅及 IP 储备⽅；此前⾏业快速扩张下

存在供给过剩、流量分流及同质化开发问题，规范化要求将加速低质、重复供给出清，平台流
量与项⽬资源进⼀步向具备优质内容⽣产与稳定产能的头部制作⽅集中；版权保护强化也有望
提升侵权成本、压缩盗版空间，优质 IP 与正版版权资源的商业价值进⼀步提升。
其⼀，爱奇艺创始⼈、 CEO 龚宇在 2026 爱奇艺秋季悦享会上表⽰，得益于 AI 技术发展，短剧
迎来爆发式增⻓， AI 打破传统影视⾏业的⾼⻔槛、缩短制作周期，成本下降后创作者与作品
数量⼤幅增⻓，推动⾏业步⼊正向循环；爱奇艺将借助 AI 探索新型内容作品拉动⽤⼾消费。
其⼆，据报道，国内⾸部 AI 超写实院线电影定档 10 ⽉ 23 ⽇登陆全国院线， AI 视频概念持续上
⾏。其三，海外 AI 应⽤催化共振 —— ⾕歌云发布通⽤⼯作智能体 Gemini Agent ， Meta 发布⾸
款⽆摄像头 AI 眼镜， AI 应⽤密集落地强化了市场对传媒内容降本增量的预期。

### Page 6

中国银河证券表⽰，⾏业供给侧正在适应观影群体新偏好，暑期档票房同⽐回升已验证优质内
容仍能有效带动观影需求，关注后续影⽚制作及排期上映对⼤盘的修复；头部 IP 续作及系列化开
发持续推进，有望带动授权、衍⽣品等产业链价值进⼀步释放。
固态电池 / 锂电池：政策、排产双催化，时代万恒 5 连板领涨
电池链是当⽇另⼀条持续活跃的主线。时代万恒开盘⼀字涨停实现 5 连板，近 5 个交易⽇累计涨
超 60% ；紫⽵⾼科 4 连板，领湃科技 20cm 涨停收获 2 连板，丰元股份、雄韬股份 2 连板，⼒王股
份 30cm 涨停，科恒股份 20cm 涨停，天赐材料、科⼒远涨停；宁德时代盘中涨超 5% 。
催化来⾃政策与排产两端。政策端，⼯信部等七部⻔联合印发的《新型电池产业发展 " ⼗五五 " 规
划》明确到 2030 年全固态电池初步实现规模化应⽤、⻓寿命锂电池循环寿命达 1.5 万次等⽬标，
并推进全固态电池与钠电池等重点领域标准研制；财政部明确 2026 ⾄ 2028 年底钠离⼦电池、固
态电池等创新电池品类免征消费税。

排产端，产业链最新调研显⽰， 10 ⽉ 6 家电池样本企业计划排产合计 214.6GWh ，同⽐⼤增
57% 、环⽐增⻓ 4% ，正极、负极、电解液排产同⽐增幅均逼近或超过 48% ，景⽓度获数据印
证。

### Page 7

国⾦证券认为，锂电板块当前处于上⾏周期、量价⻬升的繁荣阶段，固态电池步⼊ " 实⻋验证 + 中
试放⼤ " 的⼯程化关键期，产业化有望由单点突破⾛向系统推进。国信证券表⽰，锂电基本⾯受
益于出⼝需求，⻰头超跌后有望价值回归，排产延续向上，储能需求持续向好，电动⻋出⼝快
速增⻓叠加内销单⻋带电量提升，隔膜、铜箔维持紧平衡，推荐关注电池、隔膜、铜箔、固态
电池与钠电池等⽅向领先企业。
国投证券指出，固态电池 2027 年有望装⻋，⽐亚迪将于明年推出⾸款搭载固态电池技术的量产
⻋型，奇瑞全固态电池计划 2027 年上⻋验证；海外⽅⾯松下 2026 年四季度启动全固态出样、三
星 SDI 维持 2027 年下半年量产⽬标，固态产业化国内外共振。
平安证券认为，固态电池是突破电池性能瓶颈的重要技术，具有⾼能量密度和本征安全性，⻓
期前景⼴阔，电池、设备及材料环节参与者均有跟踪价值。
算⼒硬件延续调整
前期拥挤的算⼒硬件⽅向延续弱势，铜箔 / 覆铜板、玻纤、 PCB 、电⼦化学品、半导体等⾏业跌
幅居前。 PCB 概念中⾦安国纪回封跌停。⻛电设备板块震荡下挫，⼤⾦重⼯跌停，海⼒⻛电、

天顺⻛能等下挫。

### Page 8

隔夜海外 AI 情绪扰动仍在 ——OpenAI 披露截⾄ 9 ⽉底年化营收约 500 亿美元、较市场此前⼴泛报
道的约 700 亿美元低约 200 亿美元，虽主要源于统计⼝径差异，仍引发市场对 AI 基建资本开⽀落
地节奏的阶段性担忧。
随后，今天⼜有报道称， OpenAI 预计，到今年年底，其年化营收将达到或超过 700 亿美元，主
要得益于企业业务的增⻓。
分析师表⽰，过去 18 个⽉⾥，围绕⼈⼯智能已经出现了 “ ⼀波⼜⼀波的坏消息 ” 。通常情况下，这
些负⾯消息会在⼏天后逐渐平息，市场⼜会重新转向乐观。但这种情况正在发⽣变化。这些负
⾯消息出现的间隔越来越短，频率也越来越⾼。这已经开始对投资者情绪产⽣⼀定影响，这是
⼀个值得担忧的问题。

只能依靠 P 挡应急制动。当⽇， “ 尊界 V800 刹⻋踏板⽀架断裂 ” 等消息冲上热搜，事件引发⼴泛关
注。
10 ⽉ 8 ⽇晚间，尊界汽⻋官⽅微博发布 “ 关于尊界汽⻋制动踏板⽀架底座相关问题的情况说明 ” 。
说明称，尊界汽⻋的制动系统，按照⾼于国家标准（ GB 21670‐2025 / GB 7258‐2026 ）、⾼于
⾏业标准（ QC/T788‐2018 ）的要求完成全流程开发与验证。⾃⾸批⻋辆交付以来，在⽤⼾实际
使⽤场景中，未发⽣制动踏板⽀架底座断裂故障。尊界汽⻋表⽰，⽤⼾安全永远是第⼀优先
级。即便是⾮标准的极端测试⼯况，公司仍希望为⽤⼾提供更⾼的安全冗余：将进⼀步优化该
部件设计，并为已交付⽤⼾提供免费升级选择。
在江淮汽⻋因为尊界 V800 刹⻋踏板⻛波连续跌停之际，港股的⼩⽶集团却因为⼩⽶汽⻋锁单等

利好消息持续上涨。

测⽼视频 ” 刷屏。

锁单进展，从 9 ⽉ 7 ⽇正式发卖到 10 ⽉ 7 ⽇，上市满 30 天的锁单总量已经突破 7 万台。
摩根⼤通在关于中国⻋企全球化趋势的深度观察中指出，中国汽⻋出海逻辑已迎来质的转变

## Document 232: Energy shock_ Dashboard 2026 vs. 2022 - October 2026 (2026-10-02)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Energy shock_ Dashboard 2026 vs. 2022 - October 2026 (2026-10-02).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-02
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Energy shock: Dashboard 2026 vs. 2022
10/2/2026 2
Will the same causes produce the same effects? In other words, will the war in Iran and the resulting
surge in oil and gas prices lead to an inflationary shock comparable to that seen in 2022? Will their
negative effects on growth be the same as those of the war in Ukraine and the subsequent energy
shock?
We have selected a set of indicators to track the impact of this new energy shock — caused by the war

in the Middle East — on activity and prices in the Eurozone, the United States, oil and gas markets and
emerging countries, and to see how much the current situation resembles that of 2022 at the outbreak
of the conflict in Ukraine.
This dashboard features charts and comments that will be updated on a monthly basis for as long as
necessary.
Overall, based on data available through September 2026, economic activity continues to show
resilience. The high levels of business climate surveys illustrate this well. Household confidence,

however, is more vulnerable and sensitive to energy price pressures. Financing conditions in emerging
economies also continue to hold up well against the shock (much better than in 2022) as well as
against the tensions in DM bond markets, which intensified this summer, partly in the wake of the
reinforcing upward pressure on energy prices. Higher energy prices are also fueling inflationary
pressures and pushing inflation even higher. In particular, Eurozone inflation rose by 0.6 percentage

points to 3.8% year-on-year (September flash estimate), reaching its highest level in three years.
ECONOMIC RESEARCH
Eurozone: Marked increase in inflation in September 2026,
to a 3-year high
Source: S&P Global, Eurostat, European Commission, Macrobond, BNP Paribas
Change since m = 0 = February 2026 / February 2022. Unit: inflation change in percentage point, point for confidence surveys.

## Document 243: First Impressions_ NZIER Quarterly Survey of Business Opinion, September quarter 2026 (2026-10-09)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ NZIER Quarterly Survey of Business Opinion, September quarter 2026 (2026-10-09).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#businessconditions #newzealand
First Impressions: NZIER Quarterly Survey of Business Opinion,
September quarter 2026
By Michael Gordon
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
08:35 October 06 2026 Share
Businesses are feeling more optimistic about the months ahead, but current conditions
remain tough for many industries. Cost pressures eased off a little compared to last quarter.
Key results (seasonally adjusted)
General business situation: +40 (Prev: +14)

### Page 2

Trading activity, past three months: -1 (Prev: +1) 
Trading activity, next three months: +15 (Prev: +11) 
Average selling prices, past three months: +27 (Prev: +37) 
Average selling prices, next three months: +45 (Prev: +51)

The NZIER’s quarterly survey of business opinion showed a strong lift in general confidence in
September, despite resurgent fuel prices and the uncertainty around the looming election. Firms were
more confident about their prospects for the next few months, even though their recent performance
remained rough in many industries. 
The survey was held over a relatively compressed period (9 to 28 September), with around 40% of
responses coming back on the first day. Later responses were less confident, as the resurgence in
world oil prices was finally reflected in prices at the pump over the course of the month. 
General business sentiment rose to a net 40% positive compared to a net 14% in June, returning to
around the levels seen in the December quarter last year (before the Middle East conflict). However,
the own-activity measures, which tend to have a closer correspondence with GDP growth, were more
subdued. Trading activity over the last three months was slightly weaker than in June, while
expectations for the quarter ahead saw a more modest four-point increase to +15%. 
Other forward-looking measures were also up for the quarter. Hiring intentions rose strongly to a net
23% positive (although past employment was down). Notably, firms continue to report that workers are
becoming harder to find, despite the ongoing rise in unemployment over the last couple of years. That
said, the proportion of firms citing labour as a factor limiting output declined this quarter. Investment
intentions picked up, more so for plant and machinery than for buildings. 
The results were mixed across industries. Manufacturing and services were more upbeat for the
months ahead but reported substantially weaker performance over the last quarter. In contrast, builders
and merchants reported both stronger past results and greater optimism about the near-term outlook. 
The pricing measures in the survey were down compared to June – not entirely surprising as fuel
prices were lower on average through the September quarter compared to the June quarter. The
notable exception was merchants, who expected both higher costs and prices for the quarter ahead. 
The implications for the RBNZ ahead of its 28 October policy review are unclear – there is certainly no
smoking gun in this report that would demand that the RBNZ abandon the “pause” strategy
communicated at the September MPS. Optimism among businesses remains high despite the obvious
headwinds, but there is still a wide gap between hopes and reality. The pricing measures have eased
compared to last quarter, but that may simply confirm the RBNZ’s expectation that annual inflation will
drop from the 4.1% recorded last quarter as fuel prices eased. The September quarter CPI, released
on 22 October, may be more informative in that respect. 
Michael Gordon, Senior Economist
P: +64 21 749 506

2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
TODAY’S INSIGHTS
Financial Markets
• Share markets generally stalled across the globe. In the US,
stocks partially retraced gains from earlier in the week, with
the S&P 500 (–0.2%), Dow Jones (–0.7%) and NASDAQ
(–0.2%) all declining. Stocks fared worse across the pond,

with the Euro Stoxx 50 effectively unwinding all gains in
recent days (–1.5%), marked by chunky declines in Germany’s
DAX (–1.4%) and France’s CAC (–1.2%), alongside a larger
decline in London’s FTSE 100 (–0.8%). Stocks were also
weaker across much of Asia, falling in Tokyo (–0.9%), Hong
Kong (–0.6%) and Seoul (–2.0%). The ASX 200 only edged
slightly lower, down –0.1%, but futures markets are pointing
to a weaker open this morning.
•  The treasury yield curve steepened overnight. Gains in the

shorter-end saw 2-year yields fall 3bps to 4.77% – this was
despite a strong lift in consumer inflation expectations and
a hawkish FOMC Minutes, with market pricing for further
rate hikes from the FOMC instead softening at the margin.
Meanwhile, elevated yields at the long end drew very strong
demand at the latest 10-year auction, stabilising 10-year
yields at around 5.28%.
•  Bonds were more varied elsewhere, with Bunds bull
steepening amid a steady long-end, while Gilts bear

steepened amid a 6bp lift in the 10-year. ACGBs were
broadly steady during local trading, with yields inching only
slightly higher, though that gave way to gains in futures
trading overnight. Swaps markets continue to have around
6-7bps priced in for the RBA’s November meeting, while a
rate hike in December is seen at closer to 50/50 odds.
•  The USD bounced back overnight as the DXY rallied to an
intraday high of 102.5 before settling 0.4% higher at around

102.3. The Euro led the way lower, falling 0.6% versus the
greenback to just under USD1.12, back around its recent
lows. The Sterling shared a similar fate, down 0.5% toward
the USD1.321 level, remaining largely rangebound between
USD1.32-1.33 since late September. The Yen bucked the
wider trend, holding broadly steady. The Aussie dollar
meanwhile fell 0.3% to around USD0.696, unwinding gains
from earlier in the week.
•  Crude oil prices remain elevated as markets try to evaluate

the net impact of opposing forces – signs of recovering flows
of oil out of the Middle East is a positive, but reports that
Iran has increased the number of attacks on ships transiting
the Strait of Hormuz has kept a floor under prices. Brent was
up 0.5% to US$101.1/bbl overnight, still unable to break
sustainably under the US$100/bbl mark as of yet. Copper
prices lifted 0.4% to US$14,476 on the LME, gains being
driven by risks of tighter supply amid strikes at a major

Chilean mine, though the turn in risk sentiment overnight and
firmer USD capped gains. Gold prices meanwhile fell 1.3% to
around US$4,111/oz.
International Data
In the US, the FOMC’s Minutes  from the September meeting
reinforced the hawkish sentiment present at the time of the
decision to raise the fed funds rate by 25bps to a midpoint
of 3.875%. “All” participants supported the decision to raise
rates in September, and “most” saw further tightening by

year-end as appropriate. Underscoring that view was the fact
that “almost all” participants saw inflation risks as tilted to the
upside. Importantly, the assessment of the inflation outlook at
the time had already anticipated a 0.2ppt downward revision
to PCE inflation from the BEA’s change in methodology, which
ended up being 0.3ppt in the event.
The NY Fed’s measure of consumer inflation expectations
for the year ahead meanwhile surprised to the upside, rising

from 3.58% in for to 3.90% in September, marking the highest
result so far this year. Expectations also nudged 0.1ppt higher
to 3.3% at the three-year horizon, though they were stable at
3.0% at the five-year horizon.
Local Data
The Westpac Economics New Zealand team published its
September Retail Spending Pulse , which found that retail
spending remained resilient in the face of hgher fuel prices
(see here).

## Document 304: NZ forecast update, October 2026 (2026-10-01)

Source PDF: all report pdf/NZ forecast update, October 2026 (2026-10-01).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1
Westpac New Zealand Economics
• Higher oil and refined fuels prices, a lower
exchange rate, higher long term interest rates
and weak house price trends justify a review of
our 2026 forecasts.
• We continue to see CPI inflation falling to 3.7%
y/y in the September quarter, but now think
inflation will rise to 4.2% in the December
quarter. Higher global fuel prices and a weaker
New Zealand dollar are driving these trends.
• Commodity export prices remain robust and

are reflected in a rise in our forecast for the
2026/27 Fonterra Milk Price to $9.75/kg. Our
opening bid for the Milk Price in 2027/28 is a
solid $9.50/kg.
• House prices have not stabilised as expected
in recent months. As a result, we have revised
down our 2026 house price forecast from a
small rise to a 1.4% decline.
• The combination of high fuel costs and a weak
housing market will likely crimp consumer
demand running into Christmas.
• We have modestly revised down our GDP

forecasts for the December quarter, which
reduces our 2026 growth forecast to a still
solid 2.5%. Risks appear skewed to the
downside given higher energy prices and long-
term interest rates.
• We will look at our 2027 forecasts in the
coming week.
• Our OCR forecasts remain unchanged. We see
a pause at the October meeting and a hike to
3% at the release of the December Monetary
Policy Statement.
Inflation – All gas, no brakes.
We’ve updated our near-term inflation forecasts in

response to recent global developments.
We continue to estimate that consumer prices rose by
0.7% in the September quarter, consistent with recent
monthly prices updates from Stats NZ. That would see
annual inflation ease to 3.7% in the year to September,
down from 4.1% in the year to June. We’ll confirm that
figure next week, when the final monthly prices update
for the September quarter is released on 16 October.

However, that easing in inflation appears set to be only
a temporary reprieve. We now expect consumer prices
to rise 1.1% in the December quarter. That would see
annual inflation rising to a three-year high of 4.2% by

the end of this year. The main driver of this revision are
sharply higher global fuel prices, compounded by a more
than 4% fall in the New Zealand dollar since the start of
September. Together, those developments have already
pushed local petrol prices up around 50 cents / litre over
the past month, with the nationwide average price of 91
unleaded reaching a record $3.53 / litre earlier this week.

RBNZ target range
Our updated year-end inflation forecast is above the
RBNZ’s September projection of 3.9%. In a recent
presentation, Governor Breman noted that the rise in oil
prices meant the risks to the RBNZ’s near-term inflation
forecast were to the upside. However, she also noted that

the RBNZ’s focus was on the medium-term outlook.
We haven’t changed our quarterly inflation forecasts for
2027 at this stage. However, the upward revision to our
forecast for the December quarter means annual inflation
is set to remain firm through most of 2027, consistent
with the RBNZ’s forecasts. We will review our 2027
forecasts for inflation and growth more comprehensively
next week when we will also review our financial forecasts
with the release of the Westpac Market Outlook.

A more tepid housing market.
We had expected the housing market to stabilise through
the second half of 2026. However, recent trends have
disappointed. House sales volumes continue to weaken,
and the median number of days required to sell a house
has lengthened to 48 days. Not surprisingly, therefore,

average house prices have continued to nudge lower
despite more robust conditions in those areas – mostly
in the South Island – that are most exposed to the well-
performing export sector. The latest indicators suggest
no imminent change in this trend.
Given these weak trends, our previous forecast for
essentially flat house prices through the September and
December quarters now looks too optimistic. We have
revised down our house price forecast for the 2026 year

from a rise of 0.6% y/y to a fall of 1.4% y/y on a national
basis. This is consistent with quarterly falls in house
prices of 0.5% and 0.8% q/q over the September and
December quarters.
The still weak labour market, higher mortgage rates and
still moderate employment growth all point to a weak
housing market for the foreseeable future. Risks remain
tilted towards another weak year in 2027. This would
especially be the case if increased taxation of capital

gains on investor housing were to occur as a result of the
outcome of the 2026 General Election.

It’s likely to be the case that there will be divergent trends
in the housing market across the country. Income and
employment trends in tourism and agriculture dominated

regions are expected to remain robust. Our upgrade to
the 2026/27 Milk Price released in our recent Agri Bites
report emphasises this point. But it looks like it will be
a while before investors and homeowners in the major
metropolitan areas in the North Island will be feeling
wealthier through a rising home value. On balance this
is likely to crimp consumer spending attitudes through
the end of 2026 and might moderate the recovery in the
construction sector that is underway.

Growth forecasts moderated a touch.
Our short-term growth forecasts still appear to be in
the ballpark of what seems likely given recent trends.
Recent consumer and business confidence surveys have

suggested resilience of the economy to recent rises in
energy prices and long-term interest rates. However, it’s
likely the rise in energy prices in particular will eat into
consumer spending appetites towards the end of 2026
should current levels of fuel prices be sustained.
We have knocked off a modest amount off our forecast
for consumer spending in the December quarter to
reflect these risks and will look more closely at the
implications for 2027 growth in coming weeks. Certainly,

the large rise in long term interest rates may be relevant
for 2027 growth trends. The election outcome introduces
two-sided risks to the 2027 growth outlook that are hard
to quantify at this early stage. We currently expect 2.5%
y/y growth for the 2026 year.
We don’t see a need to change our labour market
forecasts for 2026 at this point. Signs continue to
accumulate suggesting strengthening employment
growth – although it will be a while before a noticeable

drop in the unemployment rate is evident.
Implications for the OCR.
We are leaving our forecasts of the OCR unchanged.
Since the July 2026 Monetary Policy Review, we have
anticipated the RBNZ will slow the pace of OCR increases
now the 3% neutral level (in the RBNZ’s view) is coming
into view.
The RBNZ sent a clear signal that they expected to pause
in October and raise the OCR just one more time in
December. We still think this is the most likely scenario.

The recent data has boosted the short-term inflation
profile but at this stage there is little evidence of rising
inflation expectations or more persistent inflationary
pressures. There are some risks to the growth outlook –
albeit two sided – to consider. Taking the time to assess
the impact of the OCR rises to date and the strategy going
forward makes sense to us. We don’t see a shift in the
MPC’s strategy in October.
We continue to think it most likely that the RBNZ will

have more work to do in 2027. However, there is a lot
of water to go under the bridge before then. The OCR
increases that have occurred to date, and the one further
expected increase in December will position the MPC
well to respond to conditions in 2027 as they unfold.
The unexpected rise in long term interest rates that
has occurred in the last 6 weeks or so has tightened
monetary conditions and has at least offset the easing in
conditions coming from the weaker exchange rate over

the same period.

## Document 349: The Wrap on Asia_ Asia’s goods disinflation over_ (2026-10-10)

Source PDF: all report pdf/The Wrap on Asia_ Asia’s goods disinflation over_ (2026-10-10).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-10
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
1
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WRAP ON ASIA
Mapping out Asia ...
Illiana Jain
Economist, Westpac Group
P: +61 403  908 032
E: illiana.jain@westpac.com.au
• For two decades, lower labour costs across Asia helped
suppress global goods inflation.
• Much of that disinflationary pulse originated in North Asia,

where production was increasingly offshored to lower-cost
labour markets.
• Today, ageing populations and rising incomes are lifting
labour costs across much of the region.
• While production continues to migrate towards South
and Southeast Asia, the next phase of manufacturing is
increasingly driven by capital, technology and power rather
than low-cost labour.
• As a result, labour costs are unlikely to provide the same
disinflationary impulse that supported goods prices over the

previous decade.
• Technology has historically provided a source of disinflation
as production costs fell and competition intensified.
• Asia sits at the centre of the AI supply chain through firms
such as TSMC, Samsung and SK Hynix, which produce
critical semiconductors and memory chips used in AI
infrastructure.
• Rising costs for electricity and critical minerals are increasing
production costs across the sector.
• Strong demand has allowed firms to pass these costs on

while maintaining healthy profitability. SK Hynix’s net profit
rose from ₩19.8tn to ₩42.9tn (+117%yr).
• Firms are also competing on performance and technological
capability rather than price, allowing technology-related
inflation to emerge for the first time in years.
• Many Asian economies remain reliant on imported energy,
leaving producers exposed to higher oil and LNG costs.
• Semiconductor manufacturing and the broader AI
infrastructure build-out are highly energy intensive.

Expansions by TSMC, Samsung and SK Hynix are increasing
electricity demand across North Asia while data-centre
investment adds a further source of demand.
• As technology firms compete with traditional manufacturers
for power, energy costs are becoming an increasingly
important part of the production process.
• Rising production costs are therefore being driven by both
higher energy prices and stronger underlying demand for
electricity.

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WRAP ON ASIA
• Chinese producer-price deflation remains evident across
parts of traditional manufacturing, particularly sectors
facing persistent overcapacity such as automobiles
• Historically, Chinese producer prices acted as the pricing
anchor for much of Asia’s manufacturing sector.

• What has changed is that parts of the region are increasingly
specialising in industries where China is not the dominant
supplier.
• Strong demand for semiconductors, memory and AI-related
goods is allowing firms in Taiwan and South Korea to set
prices according to their own market conditions despite
ongoing weakness in Chinese producer prices.
• China’s deflationary pulse remains important, but it no
longer guarantees lower prices across the rest of Asia.

• Rising costs were previously offset by production shifting to
lower-cost economies elsewhere in Asia.
• Production continues to migrate towards Vietnam, India,
Indonesia, Malaysia and Thailand, helping contain costs.
• However, these economies are unlikely to replicate the scale
of China’s integration into global manufacturing during the
2000s.
• High-value production remains concentrated in North
Asia while firms increasingly value resilience, security and

specialisation alongside cost minimisation.
• Future supply-chain shifts may therefore help limit
cost pressures, but are unlikely to generate the same
disinflationary pulse seen over the last two decades.
• For much of the past decade, falling goods prices helped
offset elevated services inflation across developed markets.
The forces that drove that disinflation are now becoming
less powerful.
• While Chinese deflation and production relocation to lower-

cost economies will continue to provide some support, they
are unlikely to fully offset these pressures.
• As a result, imported goods may provide less assistance in
bringing inflation back to target than they have in the past.
• This leaves central banks increasingly reliant on containing
domestic inflation pressures, particularly in services.
• As a result, achieving inflation targets may require a longer
period of restrictive monetary policy than has historically

been the case.

### Page 1

Trần Đức Anh 
Giám đốc Kinh tế Vĩ mô & Chiến lược thị trường 
anhtd@kbsec.com.vn 
07/10/2026

Triển vọng TTCK 4Q2026 
Tích lũy trong vùng nhiễu động

Chúng tôi dự báo vùng điểm hợp lý của chỉ số VNIndex thời điểm cuối năm 2026 
xuống 1,800 điểm, giảm mạnh từ mức 1,950 điểm đưa ra trong báo cáo gần nhất. 
Vùng điểm này tương ứng:

- Tăng trưởng EPS 2026 so với cùng kỳ của thị trường chung ở mức 33% 
(tăng mạnh so với mức 25% của báo cáo gần nhất), chủ yếu nhờ kỳ vọng 
vào các chính sách thúc đẩy kinh tế của Chính phủ, và lợi nhuận tăng 
trưởng đột biến ở VIC (xem thêm phần Triển vọng hoạt động kinh doanh).

- Mức P/E hợp lý của chỉ số VNIndex thời điểm cuối năm ở mức 13.2 (giảm 
nhẹ so với mức 13.5 thời điểm hiện tại - phản ánh quan điểm các yếu tố 
rủi ro sẽ tiếp tục duy trì). Đây là mức định giá thấp so với bình quân 5 năm 
gần nhất ở 15.1. Nếu loại bỏ yếu tố đột biến ở nhóm Vingroup, chỉ số P/E 
điều chỉnh hiện đã rơi xuống 10.9, thấp hơn các đáy gần nhất năm 2020, 
2022 và 2025. Trong bối cảnh kinh tế Việt Nam duy trì tốc độ tăng trưởng 
cao, lợi nhuận các doanh nghiệp niêm yết liên tục bứt phá, vùng định giá 
này mở ra cơ hội đầu tư trung/dài hạn. Chúng tôi cho rằng ngay khi các rủi 
ro vĩ mô quốc tế (xoay quanh vấn đề Iran), và trong nước (xoay quanh vấn 
đề lãi suất) hạ nhiệt, thị trường chung sẽ có cơ hội tái định giá lên mức cao 
hơn đáng kể so với thời điểm hiện tại. Dù vậy, khác với báo cáo gần nhất 
vào tháng 7, ở báo cáo này, chúng tôi đẩy lui thời điểm các yếu tố rủi ro vĩ 
mô hạ nhiệt sang năm 2027, đồng nghĩa với quan điểm thận trọng hơn về 
thị trường cho quý 4 2026.

Cụ thể, đối với vấn đề lãi suất, áp lực lạm phát lớn dần, đi kèm nhu cầu vốn cao 
phục vụ hoạt động đầu tư cơ sở hạ tầng sẽ khiến mặt bằng lãi suất duy trì ở mức 
cao cho phần còn lại của năm 2026 (dù thanh khoản hệ thống ghi nhận sự cải thiện 
đáng kể trong 1 quý gần đây). Đối với vấn đề xung đột Trung Đông, các cuộc đàm 
phán vẫn đang bế tắc và ít có khả năng dòng chảy dầu mỏ tại khu vực này được nối 
lại ngay trong năm nay.

Chủ đề đầu tư chúng tôi lựa chọn cho 4Q2026 là đầu tư công, chính sách tiền tệ hỗ 
trợ, và xung đột Iran.

I. Triển vọng TTCK 4Q2026 
TTCK Việt Nam đi ngang, giảm nhẹ 
trong 9 tháng đầu năm 
 TTCK Việt Nam biến động giằng co, giảm điểm nhẹ trong 9 tháng đầu năm với 
diễn biến có thể chia làm 2 giai đoạn chính. Giai đoạn 1 kéo dài đến gần cuối 
quý 1, thị trường đi ngang trong 2 tháng đầu, trước khi lao dốc mạnh từ cuối 
tháng 2 trước ảnh hưởng bởi xung đột tại Trung Đông bùng phát. Giai đoạn 2 là 
khoảng thời gian còn lại, thị trường xuất hiện các nhịp biến động mạnh, 
tăng/giảm đan xen, chịu ảnh hưởng bởi thông tin về giá dầu, biến động lãi suất 
trong nước, kỳ vọng về nâng hạng thị trường và các yếu tố cơ bản khác. Tính 
chung 9 tháng, VNIndex gần như đi ngang, giảm nhẹ 0.9% so với cuối 2025, 
trong khi thanh khoản bình quân toàn thị trường giảm xấp xỉ 12% so với cùng 
kỳ.

### Page 4

4

Với diễn biến căng thẳng tại Trung Đông vẫn đang dai dẳng, đồng thời dự báo 
mặt bằng lãi suất sẽ khó xuất hiện xu hướng hạ nhiệt rõ nét trong năm 2026, dù 
vẫn có những điểm sáng vĩ mô và KQKD khả quan của doanh nghiệp, các dự 
báo của chúng tôi nhìn chung có phần thận trọng hơn so với dự báo gần nhất. 
Cụ thể:

Lợi nhuận các doanh nghiệp niêm 
yết dự báo tăng trưởng mạnh

Chúng tôi điều chỉnh dự báo tăng trưởng EPS toàn thị trường cho năm 2026 lên 
mức 33% YoY, chủ yếu nhờ phản ánh mức tăng 200% kế hoạch lợi nhuận sau 
thuế của VIC (loại trừ mức tăng trưởng đột biến này của VIC, tăng trưởng EPS 
toàn thị trường ở mức 17% YoY). Đóng góp chính đến từ mức tăng của một số 
nhóm có tỷ trọng lớn: Bất động sản (+79% YoY), Nguyên vật liệu (+44% YoY) và 
Hàng tiêu dùng thiết yếu (+27% YoY). Mức tăng trưởng EPS cao, tương đồng với 
số liệu tăng trưởng kinh tế vĩ mô tích cực, là động lực quan trọng hỗ trợ thị 
trường trong trung/dài hạn.

Bối cảnh vĩ mô quốc tế còn nhiều 
bất định

Bối cảnh kinh tế toàn cầu trong giai đoạn cuối năm vẫn chịu áp lực lớn từ xung 
đột địa chính trị ở Trung Đông và động thái điều hành chính sách của Fed. Đàm 
phán Mỹ – Iran chưa đạt tiến triển đáng kể, trong khi giao tranh tiếp diễn và 
hoạt động vận chuyển dầu bị gián đoạn, làm gia tăng rủi ro thiếu hụt nguồn 
cung và khiến giá dầu được dự báo tiếp tục neo cao trong thời gian tới. Giá 
năng lượng duy trì ở mức cao, cùng với áp lực lạm phát chưa hạ nhiệt rõ rệt, 
khiến Fed tiếp tục duy trì lập trường thận trọng và không loại trừ khả năng tiếp 
tục thắt chặt chính sách tiền tệ trong quý 4.

Mặt bằng lãi suất huy động dự báo 
duy trì mức cao trong quý 4

Chúng tôi duy trì đánh giá tích cực về triển vọng tăng trưởng kinh tế Việt Nam 
năm 2026, với GDP dự kiến tăng 8.5–9.0%, dựa trên kỳ vọng cán cân thương 
mại cải thiện, dòng vốn FDI tiếp tục hỗ trợ tăng trưởng và đầu tư công được đẩy 
mạnh. Tuy nhiên, giá năng lượng là rủi ro đáng chú ý, có thể gây thêm áp lực 
lên cán cân thương mại và lạm phát, qua đó ảnh hưởng đến triển vọng tăng 
trưởng trong thời gian tới. 
Áp lực thiếu hụt thanh khoản ngắn hạn đã có dấu hiệu hạ nhiệt, trong khi áp 
lực huy động vốn trung và dài hạn vẫn hiện hữu khi nhu cầu vốn của nền kinh 
tế dự kiến ở mức cao. Do đó, nhìn về cuối năm, chúng tôi cho rằng lãi suất chưa 
thể xuất hiện xu hướng giảm rõ rệt, nhưng kỳ vọng có thể ổn định quanh mặt 
bằng hiện tại nhờ hoạt động giải ngân đầu tư công tiếp tục hỗ trợ dòng tiền và 
NHNN vẫn còn dư địa điều tiết thanh khoản trong bối cảnh áp lực tỷ giá không 
lớn và áp lực lạm phát vẫn trong tầm kiểm soát.
